  through the `ai` gate's DevTools connection (`Input.insertText`; the window's one debugger owner, `command` only while the
  gate holds it), both only while the gate is ready. While the operator has the window it is kept as a shown page
  (`setBackgroundThrottling(false)`, shown inactive if hidden: a hidden page takes no presses), restored when it is given
  back; a press that did not land answers nothing new, and the operator sees the page again. `go` loads only `https://<domain><path>` whose host
  is the domain (`sameDomainPath`); the window's navigation rule refuses any other host as before. A navigation main starts
  is bounded (`navigate`: it ends at the run's signal or after 10 s, the load then stopped); after each action the page
  settles (≤ 10 s). A file input is never listed, and a file chooser the page opens anyway is intercepted and refused
  (`Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`, logged `file-chooser`): no dialog of this computer opens. **The page is loaded afresh under
  the gate on entering** (independent review: a WebSocket, stream or timer the page held from the member's time is gone, so
  no press can write through it) **and again before the member gets the window back** when the operator did anything
  (Codex review: a write a press scheduled for later never fires in `human` mode). The page cannot ask to stay: while the
  operator has the window a beforeunload is let go (`will-prevent-unload` prevented, its dialog answered "leave"; review M).
  When that fresh load cannot be made under the gate — the DevTools connection gone, the load refused or not ending — the
  window is **closed** instead of handed back with that document (its login stays; review M, L1). The member's unsaved typing in that window is lost (the AI takes the window). Never while the person
  records (the debugger owner refuses `ai`), never while a plan's run uses the window (the shared `busyWindows`); a gate
  that attached late or an `enter` that threw is still left (`finally`).
- **The server decides** (`POST /browser-runs { host, conversationId? }` + this PC's key, before anything is read or
  started; then `POST /browser-runs/:runId/continue` + the key **before every step**): this PC (`devices.check`; on a
  continue the run's own registration, else `BROWSER_DEVICE_ELSEWHERE`), the company's mode with the member's override on and
  the domain registered exactly (`BROWSER_NOT_ALLOWED`), the AI not stopped (`BROWSER_AI_STOPPED`), the person at least
  見るだけ on the domain (`siteGrants.allows(…, 'read')`, `BROWSER_TYPE_NOT_GRANTED`: 使わない refused). A refusal at a continue
  ends the run (`refused`, a fixed reason) and the app stops before its next action. `finish` and the list are clean-up and
  never gated.
- **The lock** (D10): at most one running run per company × domain — a partial unique index on `browser_runs` (the database
  refuses a second running row), `BROWSER_RUN_BUSY` otherwise. Held until finish, a refusal, leaving the company, its lease
  (`BROWSER_RUN_LIMITS.leaseMs` 2 minutes, renewed by each continue) or the run's limit (its budget's `runMs`, 6 minutes for `standard`, whatever the renewals; "Room for a Skill run") —
  judged on every touch (a start on that domain, continue, finish, the list), never by a timer, so a crashed app frees the
  domain at the latest one lease later. A run is never taken over; a new request starts a new run.
- **Isolation** (I6): the page's text reaches the operator only, as outside text (`{ outside }`). The consultation AI gets
  fixed words (`BROWSER_BROWSE_WORDS`: 済みました／止まりました with a fixed reason) and, for bug 3 of §9, 「読めなかったもの：
  「…」」 — **its own headings** (`items`, ≤ 10 × 40 characters, given in the tool call) echoed back **by number**; never the page,
  the operator's summary or anything else it wrote. (A hijacked operator can choose which numbers and which fixed reason:
  a channel of a few bits, no text — said.) The operator's summary (`done.result`, ≤ 4000) goes to the **member's card**
  only, held in main's memory per signed-in member (≤ 100, cleared on sign-out), shown by the generic card through the
  inspection card's IPC (`browserLines:card` with the run's `bo_` id; the reference `data-browser-inspection` accepts it).
  **At most 3 read tasks per consultation turn** (`BROWSER_BROWSE_LIMITS.callsPerTurn`, independent review: the chat AI
  writes the task and headings, so it could phrase questions whose answers are those bits; bounding the calls bounds what
  it can collect). The card shows
  「AI のまとめ（サイトから読んだ内容。AI には見せていません）」, each asked heading 「読めました（AI の判断です）」 or
  「読み取れませんでした」, and the note that it is the AI's reading, not a machine check. Bug 4 (the ✗ wording) is not on this
  path (no comparison): it stays with step ⑤'s cards.
- **The run log** (I8, `browser_run_log`, append-only by trigger, once per run — `browser_runs.log_written` 0 → 1, so an
  empty first log counts): per action the page as a pattern, the action, and the label of a button or field — **the
  recording's rules**: the page script computes a separate `logLabel` exactly as the recording's script does (a field's own
  aria-label, `<label>` words, placeholder or title; a native button's own text only when it holds few elements; anything
  else only its aria-label or title; nothing from a row, list item or cell; never what `aria-labelledby` points at — the
  independent review of #846), then `browserAddressPattern`, `browserLabel`, `isBrowserAddressPattern`
  (`shared/core/browser-recording.ts`: digits, mail, addresses masked; query values never; a link's or an option's text
  never; what was typed or chosen never). The operator's `name` of an element (which may be a row's text) is never logged.
  A `go` the operator typed keeps a pattern only when the page showed that very link; otherwise every segment is `{x}` (and
  a query `?{x}`): an address it composed may hold a name (review L2). The server checks every step with
  `browserRunLogProblem` and refuses the whole list otherwise. A run that already ended (timed out, refused) still gets its
  log once. `GET /browser-runs`: the member's own runs, an owner / admin everyone's, newest first (200), with their logs;
  another company's run is the same 404 as none. (No admin page yet: "Not yet".)
- **Stop**: the turn's signal (the member's stop, closing the consultation, an administrator's stop through `chatTurns`),
  the deadline and **the AI stop, asked every 0.5 s while the run goes on** (review L5) abort the operator's step (its CLI
  killed) and every wait; the AI stop (`browserAiStopped`), a changed context
  (sign-in, the company's setting, the switch) or a closed / replaced window are checked after every answer; the window
  always leaves `ai` mode and `finish` is always sent (lost = the lease ends it). The emergency stop's `web` kind covers
  the tool (`agent-events.ts`), and the server refuses the next continue.
- **The tool** `browser_browse` (`mcp__acb_browser__browser_browse`, beside `browser_inspect`: the same gates — the switch,
  `capabilities.browserOperator`, never an unattended step, the turn's signal; `alwaysLoad`, auto-allowed, category `web`).
  Input `{ site, task, items? }`: `site` a domain in its very spelling, `task` ≤ 1000, `items` ≤ 10 headings ≤ 40. Its
  description holds shared words and the company's registered domains only (no site word: "The tools' words"). A run
  ending with `done` or `stop` puts the card's reference into the reply.
- **Remaining limits** (said): reading is free inside the domain, so a site that changes something on a GET (I2) is not
  stopped; the operator reads the page's text (D6: names of customers reach the model); a hijacked operator can still press
  anything — every write is refused, and a GET-only side effect is the I2 limit; dialogs are dismissed, so a page that
  needs a confirm to show something stays as it is; the done / stop words and the unread numbers are a channel of a few
  bits per call whose questions the chat AI writes (bounded by the 3 calls per turn; a member's approval per call was not
  added: the owner's call). Also (independent review, said): a run-log label comes from page-controlled attributes
  (`aria-label`, `title`) and `browserLabel` masks only digits, mail and addresses, so a name a site put in a button's
  `aria-label` is stored — seen by owners / admins only, as in a recording (L3); the run log is reported by the member's
  app, and `finish` keeps any well-formed steps: it records what the app says it did, not proof (L6); entering and
  leaving re-GET the address the window shows, so a page with a GET side effect can be hit once or twice more (the I2
  limit, L7).

Tests: `client/tests/browser-browse.test.ts` (switched off / a domain not registered / no window: nothing asked; each
server refusal → plain words, the window never entered; a good run: a continue before every step, the operator's actions
done, the AI's words fixed — no page text, injection text or summary —, the card only for the member, the log patterns and
labels only; a stop: fixed reason and unread headings only; a refusal at a step stops before the next action; the turn's
signal and the AI stop end the run, no card, finish told; answers naming another site, an element not shown, a password
field, an option not offered, extra keys or text around the JSON act on nothing; one run per window; the tool's input and
its words; the snapshot drops a password's value and another site's path), `client/tests/browser-window-electron.test.ts`
(a real window: the operator's real presses on a form's submit, a fetch button and a confirm button send no write; a link to
another site reaches nothing; a page of the site, back and an address of the site open; an address naming another host is
refused; the snapshot holds no password value), `browser-operator.test.ts` (the browse start is the same closed start with
its own prompt), `browser-device.test.ts` (the key rides on start and continue, never on finish),
`browser-core-words.test.ts` (the tool's description, schema and the operator's prompt name no site),
`server/tests/unit/browser-runs.test.ts` (the real PC registrations and site grants: unregistered / pending / no key,
the setting, a domain not registered or spelled otherwise, the stop, 使わない, another company; the lock — busy, the index
itself, the lease and the run's limit, leaving, final states —; every continue asks again and ends the run on a refusal —
the stop, 使わない, the setting, another PC, a stopped PC —; only the run's member; the log — kept once, who sees what,
append-only, values refused whole, a timed-out run's log, unknown reasons not kept), permission matrix rows + `CROSS`,
`company-scoped-sql.test.ts`, `jobs-schema.test.ts`. Mutation-checked: the window's write refusal (both Electron tests
fail), the continue's refusal, the AI's words, the registered-domain check, the AI stop, the server's grant, PC, stop,
lease and same-PC checks.

## The operator sees the screen (owner 2026-11-27: 「ブラウザ操作をするAIがポンコツ」)

On Lステップ (a heavy SPA) the operator fumbled the search box and never pressed the left folder list of a landing page:
it read only text, the list's rows were no "usable element" (no link or button: a click handler on the list), a Skill's
preview had the 20-step budget, and its notes were 2000 characters. The model was not the limit; what it was shown was.

- **The screen image** (`BrowserBrowseSurface.screenshot`, the shell's in `browser-window.ts`; the window tools only —
  the read-only preview of declared reads has no page and gets none): after each step's snapshot, the page's viewport as drawn
  (DevTools `Page.captureScreenshot` over the `ai` gate's own connection, only while it holds the window — `capturePage`
  failed on a window behind others in the harness), resized to at most `BROWSER_BROWSE_LIMITS.imageWidth` (1280) pixels wide, JPEG at
  `imageQuality` 70, sent beside the material in the operator's one stream-json message (`operatorInput`: one text block
  — the material — and one image block; an image over `BROWSER_OPERATOR_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` 1.5 MB is left out whole, the
  step goes on with text). None (the window could not draw one) → text only. Checked on the real CLI 2.1.329, 2.1.330
  and 2.1.332 (`claude -p … --tools '' --input-format stream-json` with an image block: the closed init unchanged, the
  model reads the picture; with `--no-session-persistence` no project folder, history line or paste-cache file was
  written under `~/.claude`), hence the floor 2.1.329.
- **Memory only, the operator only**: the image's bytes live in main for the one step and go to the CLI's stdin — never
  a file, a log, the run's log, the server, the card, the trail or the consultation AI's words (and never in the material
  either). It shows what the page shows, which the operator already reads as text: the same isolation (I6). Its prompts
  say that what the picture shows is outside text too (`OPERATOR_SCREEN_LINES`), as the JSON's `{ outside }` values are.
- **Set-of-marks on the pixels** (`client/shared/browser-screen-marks.ts`, pure): each element with a box on the screen
  gets a red outline and its `ref` number drawn **onto the captured bitmap** (`screenMarks` scales the page's CSS-pixel
  boxes by the image's width over the viewport's; `drawScreenMarks`). Decision: numbers on the picture rather than
  coordinates in the text — a model finds a number beside a box far more reliably than it maps coordinates onto a picture
  — and on the pixels rather than as an overlay in the page, so the page's DOM is untouched (an SPA re-renders around an
  overlay and its script could move or hide one). The snapshot carries each element's `box` and the `viewport`
  (`readBrowserPageSnapshot` keeps only whole numbers of a sane size); the material does not repeat them.
- **Elements that act as controls** (role `item`, `BROWSER_SNAPSHOT_SCRIPT`): after the real controls, elements with an
  `onclick` attribute, `tabindex` ≥ 0, an interactive role the control list does not take (`treeitem`, `switch`,
  `menuitemcheckbox`, `menuitemradio`), or the top of a `cursor: pointer` subtree (cursor is inherited: only the outermost
  element of each subtree is a candidate). A pointer subtree holding two or more list or tree items (event delegation on
  the list: Lステップ's folder tree) gives its items instead. Never a native control the first list left out on purpose (a
  file input, a hidden or disabled one: `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `button`, `option`, a link, `[disabled]`; Codex
  review). Deduped: never one inside a listed control, never one inside
  an `item` already listed (except an item inside an item: a folder inside its parent folder), never the page itself or
  a container over 40 % of the screen, never one without its own words; named by its **own** words (a nested item's text
  is the nested item's), in the kept caps (120 characters); at most `clickables` 200 of the 400 elements; each scan (the marked elements, the pointer
  subtrees) looks at no more than the page's first `clickableScan` 20 000 and last `scanTail` 2000 elements (and every
  open overlay's), a list's items at no more than 400; its words read in
  place (never a copy: no custom element's constructor runs, no image loads; independent review). Their run-log label is the recording's control rule — a row's, list item's or
  tree item's text never (`rowLike` now also takes `[role=treeitem]`; Codex review).
- **What matters first, then the cap** (production incident 2026-11-27: on Lステップ's route list a row's 「…」 menu showed
  ［コピーを作成］［削除］ on the screen image, but the sidebar and the rows had used up the 300 refs in document order and
  the menu, appended last, got none; the operator stopped with `need-person`). `BROWSER_SNAPSHOT_SCRIPT` gathers every
  candidate first (each kind ≤ `elementPool` 2000; an open overlay's controls and items before the page's, so no bound
  of a page's size leaves them out), orders them, and only then applies `elements` 400 / `clickables` 200. Tiers, document
  order within each: **0** inside an open overlay — the nearest of `[role=menu|listbox|dialog|alertdialog]`,
  `[aria-modal=true]`, `dialog[open]`, `[popover]` that is shown, or a class-named popper / portal / dropdown / popover
  container that floats (absolute / fixed; a trigger named like one does not count) — or inside a **floating layer new
  since the previous snapshot** (the nearest absolute / fixed ancestor with a z-index above 0 that the previous snapshot
  of this document did not see: `window.__acbLayers` in the isolated world, so a page's script cannot touch it; on a
  document's first snapshot nothing is new); **1** on the screen (`box`); **2** the rest of the page; **3** behind an
  open modal (`aria-modal` / `:modal`) — last, and never marked. The refs follow that order (an open menu's choices are
  ref 0, 1, …), so the material lists them first; the number left out is `omitted`, told to the operator as
  `page.elementsLeftOut`. **Marks**: an element whose centre an open overlay covers gets no box (`elementFromPoint`, in
  the isolated world), nor one behind a modal; the marks are drawn in reverse so the first (an overlay's) is on top. The
  prompts (`OPERATOR_SCREEN_LINES`, both operators) say the order and that the menu's elements come first. The refs stay
  stable within a step as before (one list per snapshot, `window.__acbBrowse`). Decision: a heuristic order rather than
  a larger cap alone (400 costs a few kilobytes of material; a long page can still exceed any cap), and the order says
  nothing about what may be pressed — the gate decides every write as before.
- **"It is on the screen but has no number"** (`unlisted`, both operators' answers, `OPERATOR_MEMO_KEYS`): any answer may
  carry `"unlisted":"<what it sees>"` (one line, ≤ 200, `operatorReason`'s rule; not a string → `unlisted`, the answer
  refused). The trail keeps its last such words (`BrowserTrailRun.unlisted`; the renderer's check takes one line only) and
  the card shows 「画面にはあるが押せる部品として拾えなかった（係の AI の言葉）：…」 (`BROWSER_TRAIL_WORDS.unlisted`) inside
  the collapsed memo — outside text like the rest of the trail: never the consultation AI's words or the run log. The
  prompt (`OPERATOR_UNLISTED_LINE`) says to stop with `need-person` when it cannot go on without it.
- **No write guard moves**: a press on an `item` is the same trusted press as on a button (`act`, at the element's
  centre); whatever the page's handler then sends is held or refused by the same `ai`-mode gate — every write refused in
  a read run, held and decided against the frames in a write run, a never-read address refused — and the frames, permits
  and the server's checks are untouched. Listing more kinds of elements changes what the operator can aim at, not what
  the page may send.
- **Notes** 4000 characters (were 2000; the trail on the card follows).
- **Remaining limits** (said): the picture shows the page as drawn, names and values included, which the operator already
  read as text (D6); an element covered by something that is no overlay of the list above (a non-floating banner) still
  gets its mark where its box is; a popup that is neither a listed overlay nor a new floating layer (a static block the page
  inserts in the flow) is ordered as any other element (on the screen: tier 1); an element with none
  of the signs (a framework's handler on a plain `div` with the default cursor) is still not listed — the operator sees
  it on the picture but cannot name it; capturing costs a fraction of a second per step.

Tests: `client/tests/browser-screen-marks.test.ts` (a mark at its box scaled to the image, none off the screen or without a
viewport, only sane boxes kept, the number and outline on the pixels, nothing outside the bitmap), `browser-operator.test.ts`
(the exact arguments with `--input-format stream-json`; the material as one user message; the image beside it on stdin
only — never an argument or a variable —; an image over the cap left out), `browser-browse.test.ts` and
`browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (each step's operator gets that step's screen, in a read run and a write run's preview and
work; never the consultation AI's words, the card, the run's log, the server, the site or the material; no screen → the
text alone), `browser-window-electron.test.ts` (a fictional folder tree driven by script alone: its three folders, a
clickable card and a tree item listed once each, never the list, its spans or the card's focusable part, no run-log
label from any of them, never a visible file input with a tabindex; a press on a folder reaches the page's handler; the card's
handler's fetch POST never leaves the window; the screen a JPEG ≤ 1280 wide with the mark's colour on the folder's edge
and the page inside it; the page's document unchanged by the snapshot and the screen; **the incident** — a fictional list of
a fixed sidebar and 400 rows (more than the cap) whose row 「…」 appends a floating menu at the end of the page: before it
opens every element on the screen comes before any off it and the last rows are the ones left out, counted; once open its
two choices are ref 0 and 1 with marks, the row it covers has no mark, and a press on ［コピーを作成］ reaches the page),
`browser-screen-marks.test.ts` (the first mark drawn on top; `omitted` read and told as `elementsLeftOut`),
`browser-operator-trail.test.ts` (`unlisted` on a read run's and a write preview's card, never in the AI's words or the
log; not a string refused; one line, capped; the prompts say the order and the word), `OperatorTrail.test.ts` (shown in
the memo as text). Each guard mutation-checked.

## Pressing by position (owner 2026-11-27: the operator points at the screen image, not only at a ref)

After the same incident (a menu choice on the screen image with no ref), the owner approved letting the operator press
**where the image shows something**, beside the refs. The element list stays (prioritised: "The operator sees the screen")
and a ref stays the surer way; a position is for what is visible but has no ref.

- **The answers** (`OPERATOR_ACTION_LINES`, both operators' prompts): `{"do":"click","x":…,"y":…}` in the **screen image's
  pixels** (whole numbers inside the image; never with a `ref`, `point` otherwise), and `{"do":"type","text":…}` without a
  ref — into the element that has the focus now (pressed first), the same caps as typing by ref. The material carries the
  image's size (`page.screen`); the prompt says to prefer a ref and what a refusal's `why` means.
- **The image's size is main's** (`pageWithScreen`): the shell's screenshot answers its `width` / `height`; the loop
  (`browse.ts`, `work.ts`) sets `screen` on the step's page only for an image the operator really gets (JPEG, ≤
  `imageMaxBytes`, which `BROWSER_OPERATOR_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` now reads) and a page with a viewport — a size the page claims is
  dropped. No image → no press by position that step (`point`).
- **Mapped by the parser** (`parseBrowseAnswer`): image pixel → viewport CSS pixel by the image's width / height over the
  viewport's (which takes in the downscale to 1280 and the screen's pixel ratio: the capture is in device pixels); the
  action carries the CSS point.
- **The hit-test** (`aim`, the shell's; `browseAimScript` → `window.__acbAim`, made by `BROWSER_SNAPSHOT_SCRIPT` in the
  isolated world, so the page's script can neither see nor replace it): asked by the loop before the action is logged, and
  again by `act` right before the press or the typing. **Refused** (`BROWSER_AIM_REFUSALS`, told to the operator in its
  `history` as `{ outcome: 'refused', why }`, nothing done, nothing logged): `outside` (off the viewport), `moved` (since
  the snapshot — and the screen image made right after it — the page scrolled or resized, an overlay opened or closed, an
  element shown on the screen moved more than 2 px or went, or the point is on a floating layer the image did not show:
  the operator gets the next snapshot instead; **except** when the element at the point is a listed one that held the
  point in the snapshot too — a small movement elsewhere does not stop every press —; and always when that listed element
  is no longer what it was, or an unlisted one changed: "What a press did"), `frame` (an `iframe` / `frame` / `object` / `embed` there: the hit-test
  cannot see inside it, a cross-origin one above all — every frame, the safe side), `file` (a file field or a label of one:
  no file chooser ever opens), `nothing`; for typing, `password` (a password field is never typed into by the operator),
  `file`, `frame`, `not-editable` (the focus is on no text field: `roleOf` = textbox, not disabled or read-only). A window
  without `aim` refuses both.
- **Logged as a ref's press**: the run log and the trail take the label of what the hit-test found — the nearest listed
  element containing the point, else the nearest control, else the element there, through the recording's label rules
  (`logLabelOf` / `controlLabel`: a row's or list item's text never; a link's never) — via `browseLogStep(…, aimed)`. What
  was typed is never kept.
- **Safety is unchanged**: how a press is aimed was never the security boundary. A press by position is the same trusted
  input as a press by ref; whatever the page then sends is held or refused by the same `ai`-mode gate (every write refused
  in a read run; held and decided against the frames and the server's permits in a write run; a never-read address
  refused), and the file-chooser interception stays (the `file` refusal only spares the attempt).
- **Remaining limits** (said): an element inside a shadow root is hit-tested at its host (a file field inside one is
  caught by the interception, not by `file`); a picture or canvas whose pixels change without a DOM change is not seen
  ("What a press did" compares words and the DOM); a frame of the same site is refused too.

Tests: `browser-window-electron.test.ts` (a real window: a menu choice with no ref pressed by its place on the screen image
and logged by its own label; a file field and its label refused, never pressed; a point off the image refused by the
parser and off the viewport by the hit-test; a page that moved after its snapshot refused; typing into the focused text
field, logged by its label; a password field never typed into), `browser-browse.test.ts` (the mapping and every refusal of
the parser, no press by position without the image the operator got, a page's own size dropped; the loop logs the hit-test's
label, never the typed text, and tells a refusal and why; a window without the hit-test refuses; the hit-test's answer read
strictly; the prompts). Each guard mutation-checked.

## What a press did (real-window probe 2026-11-27: a wrong row written, a press believed done that was not)

On a real Electron window with fictional pages: a list that re-sorted between the snapshot and the press made a row's
［保存］ ref write another person's row (`/p/item/33`, inside the frame `/p/item/{n}`); a banner over the button took the
press and the result was `ok`; a button that turned 保存 → 削除 in place was pressed by position and the delete sent; a save
behind `window.confirm` was cancelled by the app and the operator saw `ok`, no write, no change; a `maxlength` cut, an
empty date field and a `beforeinput` refusal were all `ok`. The operator could not tell. Fixed in the window, for every
site (no site word; the tests' pages are fictional).

- **Checked before acting on a ref** (`BROWSER_SNAPSHOT_SCRIPT` keeps, per listed element, its role, its name and the
  words of its context — the nearest row `tr` / `li` / `[role=row|listitem|treeitem|option]`, else the nearest of its
  next four ancestors with words beyond its own; compared **whole** as a 32-bit hash, never cut (independent review of
  #901: rows alike in their first 200 characters); an element with no context and another listed element of the same
  role and name cannot be told apart and is never acted on (`moved`); `__acbAim.same`): a press (`browsePointScript`:
  checked, scrolled into view, two frames, checked again — a virtual list may re-use rows on the scroll), a typing
  (`browseFocusScript`) and a choice (`browseSelectScript`) are made only while all three are unchanged; else
  `refused` / `moved`, nothing done. **A press also needs the element itself on top** where it lands (`takesPress`: the
  deepest element there, through open shadow roots, is it or inside it — never another listed element inside it — or
  inside one of its labels; its centre first, then the middle of its first line of words, so a tree's parent folder whose
  centre holds its children is still pressed on its own words); else `refused` / `covered`.
- **Checked before a press by position** (`aimAt`): a listed element at the point is compared the same way (保存 that became
  削除 → `moved`); when the page moved it is pressed only if its snapshot box held the point. An unlisted element (no
  ref: nothing to compare its words with) is refused when anything changed since the snapshot in it or in its context, or
  it (or an ancestor) was added since — a `MutationObserver` the snapshot starts in the isolated world (child lists,
  text, and the attributes that name or enable an element; past 20 000 changes every such press is refused) — or when
  the page moved at all.
- **What it did** (`BrowserActResult`, the shell's `act`; `browseHistoryEntry` puts it in the operator's next `history`):
  `outcome` `ok` / `failed` / `refused` (+ `why`); a press's `pressed` — the element its trusted click reached, judged
  when the click comes (a capturing click listener of the isolated world on the window, armed by the check right before
  the press, before the page's handlers on the target run): its role and name, and `row-same` / `row-changed` (its
  context's words against the snapshot's: a row re-used between the check and the click); `click-unknown` when no click
  was told — a page's own capturing listener on the window added earlier may stop it first, so this never means "not
  pressed" — with `pageWrite` `page-wrote` / `no-page-write` (a write the page made meanwhile, held or refused:
  `BrowserAiGate.onPageWrite`), so the operator does not press again blindly;
  `dialogs` — each alert / confirm / prompt the page opened since the previous action, with its text (≤ 200, ≤ 5), as
  `alert-closed` / `confirm-cancelled` / `prompt-cancelled` (the app's answer is unchanged: never yes — a confirm-gated
  save does not happen, and the operator now hears so; answering a dialog is not built: a Skill's steps avoid it or the
  operator stops for a person); `typed` — the field's value once the page settled against what was typed (`typedResult`:
  `value-same` / `value-cut` / `value-empty` / `value-changed` / `value-unknown`, with `value` when not the same). A
  contenteditable field's text is its `value` in the snapshot; `contenteditable=plaintext-only` is listed. **Owner
  2026-11-27: not more instructions** — these are fixed words that explain themselves; no prompt line was added for them
  (the refusal line was shortened: each window-tool prompt is 106 characters shorter than before).
- **Isolation unchanged**: the element's name, a dialog's text and a field's value are page text — `{ outside }` in the
  operator's material only, never the consultation AI's words, the card, the run log or the server.
- **Safety unchanged**: these checks spare the wrong press; they are not the boundary. Whatever the page sends is still
  held or refused by the `ai` gate and decided against the frames and the server's permits ("Writes within frames, in the
  app"). A press the last check refused is still in the run log and the trail as tried (they are written before `act`).
- **Remaining limits** (said): the check and the input event are a few milliseconds apart (a page changing in exactly
  that moment); a row whose words change by themselves (a live clock in it) refuses presses there until it is still; a
  page that recreates an element without a visible change makes a press by position on an unlisted element `moved`
  (the operator reads again); two elements of one role and name with no words around them (within four ancestors) are
  never acted on, by ref or by position; dialogs between two actions are told with the next one.

Tests: `browser-window-electron.test.ts` (a real window, the fictional `/verify` page: the re-sorted row's ref refused and
never pressed, read again it reaches the right row and says so; a banner over the button → `covered`, neither pressed; by
position, a block growing elsewhere still lets the same listed button be pressed, 保存 → 削除 in the same box and an
unlisted line whose words changed refused, never pressed; a confirm and an alert told with their text, the confirm
cancelled; `maxlength` → `value-cut`, a digits filter → `value-changed`, a date field and a `beforeinput` refusal →
`value-empty`, a contenteditable memo listed with its value and `value-same`; the read page's own confirm told; since
the independent review: rows alike in their first 200 characters and two 開く with no words around them refused; a row
re-sorted on mousedown → `row-changed`; a click the page stops in its own capturing listener → unknown, with the page's
write; another listed button moved under the point → refused; a password field's value never read back),
`browser-browse.test.ts` (the next step's history carries the refusal, the click's element, the cancelled confirm and the
cut value, the page's words as outside text, none of it in the consultation AI's words; `typedResult` as a table; the
window's answers read strictly, a beforeunload never told). Each guard mutation-checked on the real window (the identity
check, the top check, the position press's comparison of a listed and of an unlisted element, the tolerance, the dialogs,
the typed check, the whole-row hash, the twins rule, the row at the click, the snapshot box of a moved page, the page's
write count, the password read-back: each case flips); kept on purpose as a second layer: the identity check before scrolling (`press`
checks again after it).

## Writes within frames (step ⑤a of the redesign: the model and the server's state; the app's half is ⑤b)

Design memo 「ブラウザ操作：記録から自動化へ」 §3 I3–I5 and its 「止まったら・2回動いたら・同時に動いたら」 list, §6, §7-2, §8,
§12 row ⑤, D1, D10. The AI operates a registered domain freely; each write its page makes is held by the window (⑤b),
matched to a **frame** of the run — a write target (method, path pattern, fixed body fields) × at most `max` uses — and
sent **once** under a permit of the server. The state table of "Executions with several writes" assumed the writes were
known in advance; here a run holds only an upper bound per frame. **The contract as a checked model**:
`model/browser-plan/FramesLife.tla` (README "FramesLife"; R1, R2, R4, R7, R10, R14 never past a frame, R15 one write in
flight, R16 nothing after the AI stop; six `BrokenFrames*` variants); `StepsLife.tla` stays the safe pattern's model.
**Since ⑤b the app calls them** ("Writes within frames, in the app": holding the page's write, sending it through the
core's write connection, the plan card and the after-read card).

- **Pieces.** `routes/browser-frames.ts` → `services/browser-frames.ts` (**the one owner of every state move**; each
  function names its model action) → the handle's `browserFrames` (`ports/browser-frames.ts`,
  `adapters/sqlite/browser-frames.ts`, tables in `db/schema/browser-frames.ts`, **new tables only**: nothing of
  `browser_line_*` or the old path changes). Shapes: `shared/types/browser-frames.ts`. Wired in `composition.ts` with the
  Skill service (`approvedFrames`, `stillApproved`, `runCardShown`), this PC's gate, the site grants, the policy and the stops.
- **Tables.** `browser_frame_runs` (who, the registered domain, the PC, `source` `skill` | `adhoc`, the Skill's name,
  declaration hash and folder version, the plan's hash, `state` `waiting` → `open` → `locked` → `ended`, `approval` `card` |
  `pre_approved`, the Skill's approval row `approval_seq`, times; **at most one run not ended per company × domain** — a
  partial unique index, D10 — and **no new run while an ended run's write to that domain is still `permitted`** (its 5 s
  permit may still be used: two writes never travel to one site at once; the service); a trigger keeps
  the fixed columns and moves the state only forward), `browser_frames` (one row per write target and `used`; **CHECK
  `used <= max`**, and a trigger lets `used` only grow by one), `browser_frame_writes` (a row appended per permit with its
  frame, state and both clocks' readings; **at most one `permitted` per run** — a partial unique index; a trigger allows
  only `permitted` → `done` / `verified` / `not_sent` / `unknown` and `unknown` → `done` / `verified`; since "Recovering
  from a failed write" also → `refused`).
- **Where the frames come from** (`start`, `POST /browser-frame-runs { skill } | { plan: { domain, writes } }` +
  `conversationId?`, the PC's key header): a company Skill's **approved** declaration, read by the server itself
  (`approvedFrames`, never content an app sent; the very approval row it rested on looked at again inside the start's
  transaction, `stillApproved` — a mode changed in between opens nothing), or an **ad-hoc plan** checked with the declaration's own rules (`browserSkillDeclarationFrom`: limits,
  paths, `_method`, no login / CSRF field) **and one more: every path begins with a literal segment**
  (`browserAdhocWritesProblem`: the AI wrote it, so a frame is never "any path" — `/{x}`, `/{n}/…`, `/`) — **never
  pre-approvable**. The run opens at once only for a `pre_approved`
  Skill version whose text nobody changed since (`needsRunCard` false); everything else waits for the person's approval
  (owner decision D1). The answer carries the frames and `planHash` (sha256 of the canonical plan: company, member,
  domain, source, Skill, declaration hash, folder version, frames) — the card shows it — and, for a run that waits for
  its card, **a one-time approval value** (`approvalToken`, 256 bits): answered **only by the start** (never by `get`,
  a permit, a report or any list), only its sha256 stored (`approval_token_hash`). ⑤b's main process keeps it for the
  card and never puts it in anything an AI tool answers, so an AI that can call the routes with the member's session
  still cannot approve. **One waiting run per member** (partial unique index): a new start ends the older card's run
  (`superseded`); ⑤b's card calls `end` as soon as it is dismissed, so a dismissed card never holds the domain.
  Availability trade-off (said): D10 keeps one live run per company × domain, so a card left open blocks that domain for
  the others until it is approved, dismissed, replaced or 10 minutes pass.
- **Approve** (`POST …/:runId/approve { planHash, approvalToken }`, exactly these keys): only `waiting`; the one-time
  value (403 `BROWSER_FRAME_APPROVAL_REFUSED`; the run stays waiting); the hash the card showed (409
  `BROWSER_FRAME_PLAN_CHANGED`); the gate; for a Skill run the declaration **and folder version read again now** equal to
  the run's (else the run ends, 409 `BROWSER_SKILL_CHANGED`: the card showed another version) and the approval in force;
  then `open` and `runCardShown(skill, hash, files)` with the `files` **of this server-side read — never a value from the
  request** (no request field exists for it).
- **Permit** (`POST …/:runId/permit { frame, request: { method, path, fields } }`, the PC's key header; one transaction
  after the Skill's git read): time-outs first; `waiting` → `BROWSER_RUN_NOT_APPROVED`, `locked` →
  `BROWSER_RUN_AFTER_UNKNOWN`, `ended` → `BROWSER_RUN_ENDED`; a write `permitted` → `BROWSER_RUN_WRITE_IN_FLIGHT` (the
  second page write waits until the first's result is recorded); the frame's units used up → `BROWSER_FRAME_USED_UP`;
  **the held write does not fit the frame** → 409 `BROWSER_FRAME_MISMATCH` with a fixed `reason` (`method` / `path` /
  `made` / `query` / `method-override` / `fields`) — `shared/core/browser-frame-match.ts` `browserFrameMismatch`, **the one
  rule** ⑤b's app asks too: the method exactly; the path (no fragment or backslash) against the pattern — literals
  exactly, `{n}` digits, `{x}` one segment, never a dot segment or an encoded `/` / `\` —; the raw query exactly the
  frame's (none at all when the frame has none: "A write with an exact query"); the body's `_method` exactly the frame's
  (an undeclared one never passes, I4); the frame's fixed fields exactly and **no other field named** (the app sends
  the request's shape, never its other values) (nothing changes in those cases); then **the gate** — this PC (an enabled
  registration, and the run's own: `BROWSER_DEVICE_ELSEWHERE`), the company's mode and the site's level allowing writes
  (`BROWSER_NOT_ALLOWED`), the AI stop (`BROWSER_AI_STOPPED`), the person's own level 変えてよい (`BROWSER_TYPE_NOT_GRANTED`),
  and for a Skill run **exactly what it started on** — the declaration, the folder version (the steps' text: a change
  needs the card again) and the approval row (`approval_seq`: revoked, superseded or given another mode → another row),
  read again now and that row still the latest inside the transaction (`approvedFrames`' refusal, or
  `BROWSER_SKILL_CHANGED`) — **a refusal of the gate ends the run** (its
  frames vanish; lifting the cause later revives nothing). Then one unit taken (compare-and-set `used < max`) and the write
  appended `permitted`; answer `{ write, frame, permittedAt, validMs: 5000 }`. **Since "The run's promise"** a run with a
  promise is also asked, after the write ledger and before the gate, whether the write keeps it (`check`; nothing taken
  when not).
- **Report** (`POST …/:runId/report { write, result: done | not_sent | unknown, reason?, created? }`; `created`: "Writes only
  to what this run made"): never gated by the stop or the
  levels, but **`done` / `not_sent` only from the run's own PC** (its key header, as the gate checks it; else
  `BROWSER_DEVICE_ELSEWHERE` and the write settles `unknown` at its time-out); `unknown` from any session of the member.
  Judged after the time-outs. `permitted` → that result (`done` = "the site returned success"; **`verified` — "machine-checked as predicted"
  — is reserved for the safe patterns: no route sets it**); `unknown` locks the run. `unknown` + `done` → `done` (late and
  sure), the run stays locked. The same again → `same`; `not_sent` after `unknown` → `recorded` (kept unknown: it may have
  been sent); any other contradiction → 409 `BROWSER_REPORT_CONFLICT`. A reason is kept only when it is a word of
  `BROWSER_REPORT_REASONS`. **There is no "sent" state on the server**: `permitted` already means "may be on its way" (the
  server cannot see the moment a request leaves; "not sent" is the app's word, said only before it leaves).
- **Time** (judged on every touch): a write `permitted` and silent past permit + 5 s + 30 s **on both clocks** (as
  "The clock fix") → `unknown`, the run locked; a run `waiting` ends after `BROWSER_FRAME_TIMES.approve` (10 min; a Skill's `approveSkill`, 21: "Room for a Skill run"), any run
  after `run` (40 min) — `expired`, its frames gone.
- **End** (`POST …/:runId/end`): the run `ended`, its unused frames gone (the view's `left` is 0); a write still
  `permitted` is left to its report or time-out. **Leaving the company** (`memberLeft`, in the removal's transaction):
  every run of the member ends (`left`) and a write still `permitted` becomes `unknown` at once (nobody will report it) —
  also a write of a run they had already ended (its end kept).
- **Who**: a run is its member's own; anyone else's, another company's: the same 404 `BROWSER_FRAME_RUN_NOT_FOUND`
  (permission matrix rows + `CROSS`). The member's session only (no secret: the run's PC is checked at every gated step).
- **The text-changed card is shown once for the whole company** (owner decision 2026-11-25): `runCardShown` marks the
  approval row's `files_seen`, so the first member who approves the card for that version clears it for everyone.
- **Triggers**: the schema's `CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS` never replaces a trigger already in a database — a changed
  trigger body needs an explicit `DROP TRIGGER` + `CREATE TRIGGER` in a migration (as for every table here).
- **Where the member's credentials live** (why an AI on the PC cannot replay the card): the session cookies are held in
  main's memory only (`client/electron/services/session.ts`) and never handed to the renderer
  (ipc-and-main-process.md, `ipc-app.test.ts` 「auth:signIn / auth:signUp never hand the session token」); this PC's key is
  sealed by the OS keychain in `<userData>` ("This PC's registration", the app's half) and held in main; the AI sandbox
  denies reads of the app's userData (`agent-sandbox.md`, `sandbox-policy.ts` `denyRead`, `sandbox-policy.test.ts`).
  With the sandbox off for a company, the key file stays keychain-sealed; the approval value itself never leaves main.
- **Decisions (safe side)**: a unit is used by the permit, not given back on `not_sent` (a run may ask again with a new
  plan); a refused permit ends the run, also when the refusal is another PC's request of the same member; the domain lock
  is joined with step ④'s run lock since ⑤b ("Writes within frames, in the app"); `GET /browser-frame-runs/:runId` answers `no-store`.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` (a fictional site; the ad-hoc plan waits and needs exactly the card's
hash, the declaration's rules on a plan, a frame never past its max (also the CHECK), one write in flight (also the
index, and two permits racing past their git reads), nothing after unknown — also after a late done —, a silent write
unknown on both clocks and not on the wall clock alone, the AI stop / the person lowered to 見るだけ / the site lowered /
another PC refused and ending the run, the Skill's approval revoked — also between the git read and the transaction, at
the start and at a permit —, the declaration changed, pre-approved vs per-run, a text change: the card once, marked seen
only with the server's files (an extra `files` in the request refused), a text changed again while the card was up ends
the run and marks nothing, another member's and company's run not found, one run per domain, frames gone at the end,
the run's times, leaving, the reports, the triggers), `model/browser-plan/check` (FramesLife and its broken variants),
permission matrix rows + `CROSS`, `company-scoped-sql.test.ts`, `jobs-schema.test.ts`. Also: the approval's mode or the
Skill's text changed mid-run (the run ends), the card's approval re-checking the gate and the approval, an ended run's
write keeping the domain busy, leaving after ending a run with a write in flight, every trigger. Each guard
mutation-checked (about 40 mutations); kept on purpose as second layers: the service's own used-up check (the
compare-and-set `used < max` refuses the same, and the CHECK behind both) and the declaration / domain comparison of a
Skill run (another declaration is another approval row, which `stillApproved` refuses).

## Writes within frames, in the app (step ⑤b of the redesign: the operator writes; each page write held and sent once)

Design memo 「ブラウザ操作：記録から自動化へ」 §3 I3 / I4 / I6 / I7 / I8, §6 (6-1 the flow, 6-2 sending once, 6-3 the one
approval), §8, §9 bugs 1 / 3 / 4, §12 row ⑤, D1, and its two additions (追記2: on a real site, holding the page's write,
sending it once on a separate connection with the page's own login and fulfilling the page's request with the answer
minus Set-Cookie worked end to end — multipart 200, JSON 201, JSON 204, the page's flow went on). The consultation AI can
ask for a **write task** on a registered domain; the operator AI presses 「保存」 itself; every write the page makes is
held by the window, matched to one of the run's frames ("Writes within frames"), sent **once** through the core's own
write connection under the server's permit, and its answer handed back to the page. Behind the same switch as everything
else. **No site word** anywhere (a fictional site in the tests).

- **Pieces.** `client/shared/browser-frame-writes.ts` (pure: reading a held write `readHeldWrite`, `frameRequestFor`,
  `pickFrame`, `sentFields`, `createdIdOf`, `afterReadTarget` / `afterReadAddress` / `beforeReadAddress`, `topFields`,
  the operator's two prompts `BROWSER_OPERATOR_WORK_PREVIEW_PROMPT` / `BROWSER_OPERATOR_WORK_PROMPT`, `parseWorkAnswer`,
  `workMaterial`, the run log's `workWriteLogStep`, the AI's words `BROWSER_WORK_WORDS` / `workWordsForAi`, the cards
  `workPlanSentences` / `workResultCard`; the window port `HeldPageWrite` / `HeldPageWriteAnswer` / `BrowserWorkHooks`);
  `electron/features/browser/frame-writes.ts` (`createFrameWriter`: one held write at a time; no Electron);
  `electron/features/browser/work.ts` (`wireBrowserWork`: the task's flow, the cards in memory); `skill-steps.ts` (a
  Skill's `SKILL.md` / `site-notes.md` from the member's company folder at the path the frames run names — the company's or a department's, "Department Skills" —, through the allowed roots, joined by the core's rule and checked against the server's seal: "The operator works from the approved steps"); the shell's hold
  (`browser-window.ts`: `BrowserAiGate.hold`, `holdPageWrite`, `sendHeld`); the tool `browser_work`
  (`electron/browser-request/inspect-tool.ts`, registered in `agents/claude.ts`, category `web`); the core's
  `browserNeverReadFits` (`shared/core/browser-frame-match.ts`), the card row's `compared` / `by` / `mark`
  (`shared/core/browser-card.ts`), the run log's write steps (`shared/core/browser-run-log.ts` `BrowserRunWriteStep`,
  `browserRunWritesProblem`).
- **The flow** (`work`; each refusal is a fixed word, nothing sent): the switch, a registered domain, not stopped, the
  member's window and its read session, the operator, one run per window → **a Skill**: `POST /browser-frame-runs
  { skill }` (a name or a path; the server resolves it — "Department Skills" — and answers the path; the server's approved frames, its `afterRead` / `neverRead` — answered by the start only) → **the preview**
  (read only) unless the Skill version is pre-approved and its text unchanged: an operator run (`POST /browser-runs
  { host, write: true, frameRunId? }`), every write the page makes held and failed, a never-read address refused; the
  operator answers `plan` (what will be done, who else is affected, the key values read; ad hoc also the frames in the
  declaration's grammar, `browserAdhocWritesProblem` — a Skill plan names none); its `finish` is **awaited** and must be
  taken (the joined lock: the frames run cannot start while the preview's run holds the domain; Codex review) → **ad
  hoc**: `POST /browser-frame-runs { plan }` → **the plan card** (a waiting run) → ［任せる］ → **the work**: a new operator run naming the frames run, the
  page's writes handed to the writer → **the after-read** → the result card → the frames run ends (`end`) whatever
  happened (an end or a finish lost to the network is kept and sent again: "Letting a domain go"). The AI's tool call waits for the card and the work (the card up to the server's 10 minutes (a Skill's 21) counted from the frames run's start, each operator run within its budget: "Room for a Skill run"), then
  answers; the conversation goes on with its next step.
- **Who may start one** (server): `POST /browser-runs { write: true }` asks the person's own level 変えてよい
  (`siteGrants.allows(…, 'write')`, `BROWSER_TYPE_NOT_GRANTED`) and the site's level allowing writes (`BROWSER_NOT_ALLOWED`)
  before anything is read; the frames start and every permit ask it again ("Writes within frames"). A person at 見るだけ
  never gets a write task's preview.
- **One lock per company × domain across both kinds of run** (D10; ⑤a left it to ⑤b): a frames run never starts while
  an operator run holds the domain (`BrowserFrameDeps.operatorRunOn` → `browserRuns.runningOn`, `BROWSER_RUN_BUSY`); an
  operator run starts while a frames run is not ended there only when it names that run (`frameRunId`) and is its
  member's (`BrowserRunDeps.frameRunOn` → `browserFrames.holderOn`), and never while an ended run's write may still be
  on its way (`busy`); naming a frames run that holds nothing → `BROWSER_RUN_ENDED`. Wired in `composition.ts` by
  closures called only after construction.
- **The plan card (D1)** is the write card (`BrowserWriteView`, `BrowserWriteCard.tsx`, reference `bf_…` — the frames
  run's id — accepted by `readBrowserInspectionRef` and drawn by `BrowserInspectionCard`): the operator's summary, who
  else is affected, each frame in plain words with its count (`browserSkillWriteLine`), the key values read
  (「下見で読んだ大事な値」 — read by the operator AI, never machine-checked, and the card says so —, card only, never the consultation AI), ［任せる］ (`browserLines:runWrite` with the plan's hash the card shows) and ［やめる］
  (`browserLines:cancelWrite`, new; from a window showing its consultation, "A card in its consultation's own window"; the
  run ends). **The one-time approval value** stays in the work
  wiring's memory: never in a view, a tool's answer or a log; ［任せる］ is the only path to `approve` (the AI's tools
  cannot call it). A pre-approved Skill version whose text nobody changed runs without the card (its card shows the run
  and the result); a Skill whose text changed shows the card once (the server's `needsRunCard`). The card moves on in
  main by itself (approved, running, the result, ended at the card's time): a `bf_` card is read again every 2 s until
  it is finished or failed (`BrowserWriteCard`, Codex review).
- **Holding a page write** (the shell, `ai` mode of a write run: `BrowserAiGate.hold`): every non-GET of the page is
  held — never continued — after the basic rule (`decideAiWrite` with a yes-decider: https on the operated host, the AI not
  stopped, no method-override header). The body is read from DevTools Fetch's `postDataEntries` (exact bytes) or
  `postData`; a body Fetch does not give (`hasPostData` without data: a file, a stream) or over 1 MiB is **unreadable**.
  The held copy keeps the page's own headers but the connection's and the cookies (`HELD_DROPPED_HEADERS`, `proxy-*`,
  `sec-*`): its CSRF header goes as the page set it, its login is the window's cookies for exactly that address (the
  write session's `copyCookies`). **A held write is failed before the gate lets go** (`failHeld` in `setMode('human')`
  before `Fetch.disable`: Chromium would otherwise send a paused request on its own).
- **The page's original is parked before anything is awaited** (Codex review 2026-11-28, owner approved; measured on
  Electron 45 with a fictional site, `browser-write-detach-electron.test.ts`): when the DevTools connection goes, Chromium
  lets go of every request paused under Fetch, past the request hook it already passed — a `debugger.detach()`, and the
  window closing for a `keepalive` write (it outlives its page). Before this, a cut before the permit sent the page's
  unpermitted original to the site, and a cut while the run's copy was on its way or after its answer sent it **twice**
  (copy + original); a reload, a navigation or a renderer crash cancel the original (no detach) and the copy alone went.
  Now the first thing done with a page write at the request stage is `Fetch.continueRequest` pointing it at
  `PARKED_WRITE_URL` (`https://acb-parked-write.invalid:1/`: port 1 is on Chromium's unsafe-port list, so it fails before
  any connection, proxy or DNS; the page still sees its own address — a fetch's `url`, the page a form lands on) with
  `interceptResponse: true`; it pauses again with that failure (`responseErrorReason`) and only then is it held and
  decided — by the run it was parked for, only while that run's gate holds the window —, and the page gets the copy's
  answer at that pause (`Fetch.fulfillRequest`, as before). A redirect's `Location` is made absolute against the address
  the page posted to (`fromPageAddress`; at that pause Chromium would resolve a relative one against the parked address:
  a form's 303 to `/next` landed nowhere — independent review). Released at any moment from then on, the original only
  fails. Measured,
  cut before the permit / while the copy is on its way / after its answer, by detach, closing the window (keepalive),
  reload: the site gets 0 / 1 / 1 — the permitted copy once or nothing, never the original (before: 1 original / 2 / 2
  by detach, likewise for a keepalive by closing).
- **The DevTools connection gone while a write is held or parked**: the run is told (`BrowserWorkHooks.detached(
  originalMayHaveGone)`) and nothing more is sent (the write session's hook asks `aiGateReady` too). Every original was
  parked (`false`, what the tests see): the work **closes** the writer (`close()`) — a held write not sent yet is
  `not_sent` (a permit taken is reported so), one already on its way keeps its own result (the page then sees a failure);
  a write the page made whose parking Chromium had not answered yet (`parking`: the command sent, no reply), or whose
  parking Chromium refused since the connection was made (`parkingFailed`: the connection may already be going), may have
  gone out as the page made it (`true`): `abandon('detached')` as before — every held write `unknown`, the run locked, a
  permitted one reported `unknown`, never `not_sent` (independent review of #856).
- **Deciding one** (`createFrameWriter`, in this order; each `refused` / `not-sent` fails the page's request): the run
  over, locked or stopped → not sent; `readHeldWrite` — another host, a body that is not a form, JSON or multipart text
  parts, **a part with a file**, `_method` twice, **a form body with a raw `;`** (some server stacks split on it, so the
  frame could be matched on another value than the site reads; independent review) → refused; `pickFrame` — the core's one rule `browserFrameMismatch` with
  only the frame's fixed fields and `_method` (the path **with its raw query**: a frame without a query never fits one, a
  frame with one only exactly it — "A write with an exact query"), a frame with no unit left → refused, **never asked for**; an after-read "before" read; then
  `held.send(steps)`: the write session prepared, **`ready`** asks the permit once (a unit taken is counted down here,
  never given back), **`beforeSend`** synchronously right before the request leaves — the permit under `min(validMs,
  5000)` on both clocks, the run still going (the turn's signal, the AI stop, the window, the context) — then sent once.
  Not sent after a permit → `not_sent` reported. No answer → **unknown**: reported, the run **locked** (nothing more is
  held through, the operator is stopped), never sent again; the page's own retry is a new hold, refused. An answer:
  2xx / 3xx → `done`, the page gets it (`Fetch.fulfillRequest`, **without `Set-Cookie`** — dropped by the writer and again
  by the shell — nor `content-encoding` / `-length`); **a 4xx that proves it was not taken → `refused`, the run goes on;
  any other status → unknown, locked** ("Recovering from a failed write"; before it every non-success status was
  unknown), the page still gets the answer. A report the server did not record (3 tries)
  locks the run too. **One at a time**: the next held write starts only once this one's report was recorded (a promise
  chain); a write held meanwhile waits paused.
- **The operator looks again only once the page's writes are decided** (incident 2026-11-27: after a press the operator's
  next material had no write and the frame's unit already used — the permit was taken, the site's answer not in yet — so
  it could not tell whether its press had gone out and stopped). Before each look at the page in the work (`operate` →
  `settleWrites`), all within one limit — the end's 45 s (`idleMaxMs`): right after a press, typing, a choice or back,
  main watches for **`BROWSER_WORK_LIMITS.writeStartMs`** (1 s, one value for every site; never tuned per site) for a
  write the page's script starts a moment later; every held write is recorded (`idle()`: until the chain stops growing,
  so a write held during the wait counts too); **after the page got its answers it may go on** — main watches the same
  moment again for a write it makes next (and waits for that one's record), as long as it goes on — a write that came and
  went between two of main's looks counts too (`heldCount`: every write held in the run; Codex review); then **the page
  settles** (`BrowserBrowseSurface.settled`, the window's rule after an action: a moment, then until it stops loading,
  ≤ `settleMs`). **Past the limit, or the run cut** (the member's stop, the AI stop: the wait ends at once): a write still
  on its way → `abandon('timeout')` as at the end — every held write
  kept `unknown`, the run locked and closed, the operator not asked again, nothing sent again (the end then does not wait
  a second time, `gaveUp`); **none on its way** (a page that keeps making refused writes) → the operator looks at the page
  as it is. The preview holds no write and does not wait.
- **What the operator is told of the writes** (`writer.seen()` → `workMaterial`'s `writes`, `WorkWriteSeen`): each held
  write so far — the kept ones in order, then the ones **being sent** (held, not yet kept: 「送っている途中」, `sending:
  true`, `WORK_WRITE_SENDING`) — with its number, **the frame it fitted** (`frame`, null when none), **the action it
  followed** (`afterAction`: the number of the operator's history entry, which is numbered the same way; the last press,
  typing, choice or back actually done — set before it, so its own write names it; a write held during a wait, a read or
  a refused action names the press before it — independent review), the result's fixed words and, when it was not sent, **why** in fixed
  words (`WORK_WRITE_WHY_WORDS`: outside every frame, the frame used up, not this run's object, a file, unreadable,
  another host, the run over, no permit, stopped before sending). Nothing the page or the site sent (no value, path,
  status or text); the consultation AI never gets any of it. So the operator never sees a frame's `left` gone without a
  write to show, and can tell which press each write came from.
- **No press while a held write is being sent, nor right after a press whose write has not come yet — the app's rule,
  not the operator's words** (seen on a real window: a second press while the first save was on its way queued behind it
  and the site got the same save twice, with a frame of max 2; owner: guarantee it by the mechanism, do not add
  instructions). In the work, `operate` does not do a press, typing, choice or back (anything that could make another
  write) **while `writer.sending()` is above 0**, nor **when the last such action has made no write yet and a write
  comes before `BROWSER_WORK_LIMITS.pressGapMs`** (5 s after that action, one value for every site; the app watches
  until then — usually already over, since the operator's own step takes longer; independent review: a page that starts
  its write after main's moment would otherwise let a quick second press through). Reading (`go`) and `wait` pass. The refused action stands in the operator's history as `refused` with `why: 'sending'`, told in fixed
  words (`WORK_NOT_PRESSED_WHILE_SENDING` 「送っている途中なので押していません」); the next look then waits for the write as
  above. The old prompt sentence 「同じ保存を何度も押さないでください（書き込みは1回ずつしか送られません）」 is gone: it read as
  "pressing again sends nothing", which was false. **Remaining limit** (said): a page that starts its write more than
  `pressGapMs` after the press, with the operator pressing again in between, still gets the second press (each write is
  still sent once and within the frame's units). None of this changes what is sent, permitted or reported (`FramesLife.tla`'s app actions are unchanged:
  the operator is not modelled, and giving up mid-run is the model's `AppCrashes` / the server's time-out, as at the end).
- **Kept for the card** (main's memory): each write's frame line (the frame's method and pattern — never the path the
  page sent), its outcome, the site's status when not a success, what it sent (`sentFields`: one value per field, never
  a login, CSRF or session field, `isSensitiveFieldName`) and, for a success, its answer text (what `{created}` is read
  from). Never the headers or cookies.
- **The after-read** (a Skill run's `after_read`, read through the window's page-less read session, GET of exactly the
  address): a literal address is read before anything is written; a pattern address is read right before the first
  held write whose path fits it (its "before"); after the work each address is filled — `{created}` from the done
  write whose path shares the longest beginning with the pattern's part before `{created}` (exactly one; its answer's
  `id` or `data.id`, plain characters), `{n}` / `{x}` from exactly one done write's path that fits — and read again. An
  address that cannot be filled or read is **not read**, and the consultation AI hears its **pattern** (company-reviewed
  text) as 「読めなかったもの」 (bug 3), never page text.
- **The result card** (`workResultCard`): the writes and what became of each (「サイトが成功を返しました（機械で確かめた）」 /
  「届いたか分かりません（確かめていない）」 / 「送っていません」 / 「計画の枠に合わないので止めました」); each after-read address's
  top-level values **before → after** with what the write sent beside it (`compared`) and who checked it — `machine`
  (the field the write sent: ✓ equal / ✗ differs), `none` (nobody compared) — (bug 1: both sides and the ids always
  shown); the operator's checks of the Skill's 「確かめること」 before → after → 比べ元, `by: 'ai'`, ✓ / ✗; bug 4's soft words
  「「{比べ元}」と違う項目があります。意図した変更か確かめてください。」 with the operator's name for what it compares to (default
  「比べ元」); for an unknown write, 「手でやること」: what to check on the site by hand, and that nothing is sent again. The
  renderer checks each row's shape (`readBrowserCard`); the labels' words are `BROWSER_CARD_CHECKED_BY_WORDS`.
- **The consultation AI's words** (`workWordsForAi`): 済みました / 止まりました（a fixed reason, one word per cause: "Letting
  a domain go"）/ 分からない書き込みがあります / 読めなかったもの：its own
  headings by number and the unread after-read patterns. Never the page, the plan's words, a value, the hash or the
  approval value. **One write task per consultation turn** (`BROWSER_WORK_LIMITS.callsPerTurn`).
- **Stop**: the turn's signal (the member's stop, closing the consultation, an administrator's stop), the AI stop asked
  every 0.5 s, a closed window or a changed context end the run at once: the card's wait ends (the run ends, nothing
  written), a held write is not sent (`beforeSend`, `failHeld`), one already on its way becomes unknown if no answer
  comes. The window leaves `ai` mode only after the writer is idle (≤ 45 s) and every held request was failed; **a write
  still on its way when those 45 s run out** is kept `unknown` (`abandon('timeout')`: the card, the AI's words and the run
  log say so, the run locked) — never counted as done or left out (independent review of #856). ［任せる］ answered after
  the task already ended leaves the card as the task left it.
- **The run log** (I8): each operator run's `finish` carries `writes` (`BrowserRunWriteStep`: after which logged step,
  the frame or null, the method — `OTHER` for any other —, the path as the recording's pattern, `done` / `not-sent` /
  `unknown` / `refused`); the server checks them (`browserRunWritesProblem`), keeps them in the additive table
  `browser_run_writes` (append-only by trigger) and lists them with the run (`BrowserRunView.writes`, optional for an
  older server). No value.
- **Never read** (a Skill's `never_read`): the window's read rule refuses the page's and the operator's reads of the
  operated host that `browserNeverReadFits` names (its path pattern, and each query word it lists) during the preview and
  the work.
- **Remaining limits** (said): a page write whose pause Chromium reports at the very moment the DevTools connection goes
  or the gate lets go (`Fetch.disable` in `setMode('human')`) — before main has even seen the event, so it is neither held
  nor parked — goes out as the page made it, unknown to the run (Chromium lets it go; no request hook sees it again). Not
  reproducible in a test; the window of time is one message between Chromium and main; the operator reads the page and may be misled by it — what it can do is bounded by the
  domain and the frames, and its words reach only the member's card; what a write *contains* (which number, which date)
  is not checked by the machine beyond the frame's fixed fields — the plan card and the after-read card are where a
  person sees it (§8); a site that sets a new cookie on each save gets none back (decision of the runner's write
  session; 追記2 showed the real site goes on); a site's GET with a side effect is stopped only when `never_read` names
  it; `{created}` pairing by the longest common beginning is a heuristic (an ambiguous one is not read, never guessed);
  an answer that is neither a success nor a refusal locks the run (a 4xx refusal does not: "Recovering from a failed
  write"); the Skill's steps come from the
  member's own checkout (they may be older or newer than the version the server approved — the frames are always the
  server's); an upload with a file is refused (design memo 追記: the file chooser filled by the core is a later step).

Tests: `client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (a file / an unreadable or unknown body / `_method` twice / another
host refused; the frame fit, an undeclared `_method`, a query, used up; no CSRF field kept; `{created}` and the
after-read addresses; never-read patterns; the operator's answers read strictly; the writer — outside the frames and past
the max never asked or sent, the second write waits for the first's report, no answer → unknown and nothing more, a stop
between the permit and the last check → not sent and `not_sent`, a non-success answer → unknown, Set-Cookie never
handed back, no value in the log or the server's calls; the wiring — an ad-hoc plan only after the card's ［任せる］ (the
view and the AI's words never hold the value or the hash), ［やめる］, a pre-approved Skill without the card, a text change
with it, 見るだけ refused before the operator, a write outside the plan refused during the work, the after-read card's
labels and bugs 1 / 4, the AI's words without page text or injection, the preview's finish taken before the frames run
starts, a write still on its way when the wait runs out → unknown everywhere, a detach while held → unknown and never
sent, a form body with `;`, ［任せる］ after the task ended), `client/tests/ui/browser-inspection-card.spec.ts` (a `bf_` card: the key values and ［任せる］／［やめる］, then the
after-read card by itself, each item's checker), `client/tests/browser-window-electron.test.ts` (a
real window and a fictional site: a save sent once with the page's cookie and CSRF header, its 201 JSON reaching the
page without the site's cookie in the page or the window, two saves at once one after the other, an autosave outside
the plan, an upload with a file, a never-read address, a save past the frame's max and a save whose permit came after
the stop never reach the site, a lost answer reaches it once and nothing more is sent; no cookie, CSRF or value in a
permit, a report or the log; the DevTools connection detached while a save is held: neither the run's copy nor the
page's parked original arrives, the write not sent and reported so; the same file since the 2026-11-27 fix: a press whose page
writes a moment later to a slow site → the next step's material shows it done beside the used unit, a press whose write
gets no answer within the wait → unknown before the operator looks again (asked no more, pressed and permitted once), a
write the page makes as it is read → shown as being sent then done, a write the page makes after the site's answer →
waited for too, then the page settles, each write with its frame, action and reason, a fast write that came and went
between main's looks still counted (the page settles, its next write waited for), a page that keeps writing outside
the frames → the operator still looks again at the limit, `idle` covering a write held while it waits, the material's
words — each mutation-checked: no wait, no moment after the press, no idle wait, the old `idle`, no 「送っている途中」,
no give-up, no moment after the answers, no settle, no limit, no frame / action / reason, a decided write not counted;
a press while a write is being sent not done, a press right after one whose write comes later waits for it and is not
done (also on the real window, `browser-window-electron.test.ts`: a fictional save that sends two seconds after the
press, pressed again at once, reaches the site once although its frame allows two — twice without the rule), a write held
during a wait names the press before it, the member's stop cutting the wait (unknown at once);
kept on purpose (time only): the end not waiting twice after a give-up, the moment ending early once a write was held),
`client/tests/browser-write-detach-electron.test.ts` (a real window, a fictional site: a held fetch / form / keepalive
write cut before the permit, while the copy is on its way and after its answer, by `debugger.detach()`, closing the window
and a reload — the site gets the permitted copy once or nothing, never the page's original; cut before the permit the
write is `not-sent` and reported `not_sent`; a write that reached the site never says not sent; uncut, the page still gets
the site's answer, a form lands on the address it posted to with that answer, and a 303 to a relative address is followed
from the address the page posted to, by a form and a fetch — mutation-checked (the relative redirect without
`fromPageAddress` lands nowhere): holding without parking sends the original in 8 of the 9 cases), the work's
`detached` wiring in `browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (parked → not sent, not parked yet → unknown; each branch
mutation-checked; kept on purpose: the `parking` set itself, whose race no test can make),
`client/tests/browser-core-words.test.ts` (the tool, its schema and both prompts name no
site), `server/tests/unit/browser-runs.test.ts` (`write: true` at 見るだけ / on a 見るだけ site refused; the joined lock —
only the member's run naming the frames run, `busy`, a run naming nothing → ended; write steps kept, listed, a value
refused, append-only), `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` (the start's `afterRead` / `neverRead`, not in `get`;
no frames run while an operator run holds the domain; `holderOn`), `jobs-schema.test.ts`, `company-scoped-sql.test.ts`,
the IPC inventory, `model/browser-plan/check` (FramesLife unchanged: the app is stricter, README; the parking makes
the code hold the model's assumption that only the run's send reaches the site — `SendNow` alone counts `sends`). Each guard
mutation-checked (the serial chain, the last check, Set-Cookie, the frame's units, the file refusal, sensitive fields,
unknown body kinds, the card's wait, the write task's start, bug 4's words, the machine label); kept on purpose as second
layers: the writer's early "locked" check (`ready` refuses the same), the shell's Set-Cookie drop behind the writer's.


## Room for a Skill run (one budget rule; the work of an approved company Skill gets more steps and time)

A real Skill run (copy something, then several writes) does not fit the 20 steps and 5 minutes every operator run had,
shared by the preview and the work. **The one rule and every time it depends on** are `shared/core/browser-run-budget.ts`;
`server/tests/unit/browser-run-budget.test.ts` checks that they agree.

- **Budgets** (per operator run, one `POST /browser-runs`): `standard` = 20 steps, the app operates ≤ 5 minutes, the server
  holds the lock ≤ 6 minutes (unchanged: reading, an ad-hoc plan's preview and work); `skill` = **60 steps, 15 minutes,
  17 minutes** (the work of an approved company Skill); `skill_preview` = **40 steps, 10 minutes, 11 minutes** (that
  Skill run's preview; owner 2026-11-27: a heavy site's preview ran out of 20 steps). `runMs` − `operateMs` ≥ 60 s: room after the app stops operating
  for a write on its way (≤ 45 s), giving the window back and the finish. `BROWSER_RUN_LIMITS.steps` (the log kept, 60)
  is at least every budget's steps. An older app that does not know a name the server answers runs it as `standard`
  (fewer steps than the server allows: the safe side).
- **Who decides** (`browserRunBudgetFor`, asked by the server only, at the start): `skill` only for a write task's run
  (`write: true`) naming its member's frames run (`frameRunId`, the joined lock's check) whose `source` is `skill` and whose
  state is `open` (approved on the card, or a pre-approved version); `skill_preview` for such a run while that frames run
  is still `waiting` (its preview) — read from this server's own row (`browserFrames.holderOn` answers its source and
  state). An ad-hoc plan's preview (it names no frames run) and work, a read task and a run naming nothing are `standard`.
  **The preview is not the work**: `skill_preview` is an allowance of its own — the once-per-frames-run rule, its check
  and its index count `skill` only, so a preview never takes the work's run (a second preview of a waiting frames run is
  not refused: the frames run's wait bounds it). The request has no budget field (a body naming
  one is `BROWSER_BAD_RUN`). The choice is stored on the run (`browser_runs.budget`, additive with default `standard`,
  `migrateBrowserRunBudget`; its CHECK widened to `skill_preview` by one rebuild, `migrateBrowserRunPreviewBudget` — every
  row kept, the table, indexes and trigger then exactly a new database's; a server rolled back below it reading a
  `skill_preview` row fails that one run's `continue`, never another's; fixed by the trigger, which the migration replaces when the stored one differs — compared as
  SQLite keeps it, without `IF NOT EXISTS`, so a second start leaves it) and answered (`BrowserRunStarted.budget`; absent
  from an older server, and any name the app does not know, = `standard`: `browserRunBudget`).
- **Once per frames run** (independent review of #857): the first write run that gets `skill` for a frames run is its
  work; a later one naming the same frames run (a modified app; the real app never starts a second) is **refused**
  `BROWSER_RUN_WORK_TAKEN`, also while the first still runs — not given `standard` (decision: one operator run per
  frames run's work). Guards: the check at the start (`skillRunOf`) and a partial unique index
  (`idx_browser_runs_skill_once`, `browser_runs.frame_run_id`, fixed). A run that is not a write task's still starts,
  `standard`.
- **Never past the frames run** (`browserRunWindow`; independent review of #857: an approval at 9:59 and slow reads
  before the work could start a 17-minute lock after minute 13, outliving the frames run, whose later writes would be
  refused half-way): a run naming a frames run gets a hard end `ends_at` (fixed) = its budget's `runMs` from the start,
  **never past that frames run's own end** (`holderOn`'s `endsAt`: a waiting one at its card's time, an open one at its
  hour), and the answer's `operateMs` (`BrowserRunStarted.operateMs`) = its budget's `operateMs`, ending the same margin
  (`runMs − operateMs`) before that end; the app operates no longer than both. **Less than one minute of operating left**
  (`BROWSER_RUN_MIN_OPERATE_MS`) → refused `BROWSER_RUN_TOO_LATE`, nothing started; the consultation AI hears
  `BROWSER_WORK_WORDS.tooLate` (時間切れ, nothing written).
- **The server enforces it**: the lock ends at the run's `ends_at` (renewals never pass it; an older build's row: its start
  + the budget's `runMs`), and every
