  `continue` takes one of its steps (`browser_runs.steps`, compare-and-set `steps < max` with the lease; the trigger lets
  it only grow by one while running): past them the run ends `timed_out` / `rounds` and the domain is free.
- **The app follows the answer** (`work.ts`): each operator run's steps and its deadline (its own start + `operateMs`) come
  from the run's budget — a long preview takes nothing from the work. The card waits for ［任せる］ until the server would end
  the waiting frames run: `browserFrameApproveMs` (10 minutes, a Skill's 21) counted from just before the frames run was asked
  for (`frameAskedAt`; a Skill's frames run is made before its preview, so the preview's minutes count), never longer.
  `BROWSER_BROWSE_LIMITS.rounds` / `totalMs` are the `standard` budget's (reading is unchanged).
- **The frames run's times** (`BROWSER_FRAME_RUN_TIMES`, the server's `BROWSER_FRAME_TIMES` takes them;
  `browserFrameApproveMs(source)` for the server's `endsAt` and the app's card alike): a waiting run ends 10 minutes after
  it was made — **a Skill's 21** (made before its preview: the preview's 11 and then the card's 10) —, any run 40 minutes
  after (30 before `skill_preview`). A Skill run whose work starts right after an approval at the card's last moment fits
  whole (21 + 17 + 1 ≤ 40); one that starts later gets what is left, above. A preview started at once keeps its whole
  budget (capped at the waiting frames run's end like any run naming it).
- **The AI's tool call** waits for all of it (a Skill's preview ≤ 10 and the card until minute 21, the work ≤ 15 minutes,
  plus the after-read): the
  in-process MCP tool of the consultation has no time limit of its own in the app.
- **Not changed**: `FramesLife.tla` (times are abstract there; the steps' budget is not one of its rules), the frames,
  permits, reports and every gate.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-run-budget.test.ts` (the rule as a table, `skill_preview` for a waiting Skill run only;
the times agree — a whole preview and the card's 10 minutes in a Skill's wait —; the numbers),
`server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` "room for a Skill run" (the real frames and operator runs services: a per-run
Skill's preview `skill_preview` with its 40 steps and 10 minutes, its frames run still waiting after a whole preview, no
`skill` run counted, and its approved work 60 steps; a pre-approved Skill's lock lasting 17 minutes; an ad-hoc plan's
work, a read run naming an open Skill run and a run naming nothing standard — 20 steps, 6 minutes; no budget field in the
request; the trigger keeps the budget, the frames run, the end and the step count; `skill` once per frames run, also by the
index; a work started late ends with the frames run, too late refused), `server/tests/unit/browser-runs.test.ts` (20
steps then `rounds`; a database from before the budget reads `standard`, takes `skill_preview` and no unknown name, and
ends with the same table and trigger as a new one; a second start does not drop the trigger),
`client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (the work's steps follow the server's answer — 60 for `skill`, 20 for none,
`standard` or an unknown name —; the deadline from the run's own start and never past the server's `operateMs`; 時間切れ
words; the card's wait counted from the frames run's start — a Skill's card still up after a preview as long as an
ad-hoc card's whole time). Each guard mutation-checked (28 mutations, all caught).

## Recovering from a failed write (a write the site refused; the person's answer about a write in doubt)

Before this, any answer of the site that was not a success made the write `unknown` and locked the run, so a form the
site rejected (a missing field, an expired login) ended a whole Skill run; and a write in doubt could only be checked by
hand. Two changes, each on the safe side.

- **A refusal is not a doubt — inside its own run** (`shared/core/browser-frame-match.ts` `browserWriteAnswerKind`, the one
  rule the writer asks; **since "Sent is not done"** a refused write nobody read again and found unchanged is a doubt for
  every *later* run, and its words never say "not saved" from the status alone): 2xx / 3xx → `done`; **only the listed 4xx** (`BROWSER_WRITE_REFUSED_4XX`: 400, 401, 403, 404, 405, 410, 411,
  413, 414, 415, 419, 422 — a bad or invalid request, the login or CSRF value run out, not allowed, no such address or
  method, too large, the wrong body type) → `refused`; **everything else stays `unknown`** and locks the run as before:
  every other 4xx (an allowlist, independent review of #859: 408 timed out, 409 a conflict after a partial or concurrent
  change, 424, 425, 429, a proxy's 460 / 463 / 499 … do not prove nothing changed), every 5xx, no answer, a time-out, any
  status outside 200–499. This reverses the runner's 6b-2c rule "every non-success is unknown" for the listed codes
  only. A classic form whose validation error is a 302 back to the form is still `done` (a redirect is an answer the site
  took): this recovery is for answers like 422 / 419 (a page's own fetch / XHR).
- **A refused write** (`frame-writes.ts`): reported `refused` (a sure answer: only from the run's own PC, like `done`),
  kept for the card and the run log as `site-refused` (the log's outcome `refused` stays "refused by the app before
  asking"), and the page gets the site's answer, so its own error shows. **The run goes on** (no lock): the operator may
  correct the form and press 「保存」 again. That is **a new page write**: held, matched, its own permit and **one more
  unit of the frame** — a refused write's unit is never given back, so the frame's `max` counts every try. Nothing is
  ever resent by the app. A report the server did not record still locks the run, and that write is then kept `unknown`
  (an older server knows no `refused`); a run log a server refuses as a bad run while it names `site-refused` is sent once
  more with those steps `unknown` (independent review of #859; deployment.md "the server before the app").
- **The server** (`services/browser-frames.ts`): the write state `refused` (`permitted` → `refused`; a late one after
  `unknown` → `refused`, the run stays locked; never `done`, so nothing counts it as done) and the run step outcome
  `site-refused`. Both tables' CHECKs could not widen in place: `migrateBrowserFrameWriteRefused` and
  `migrateBrowserRunWriteSiteRefused` rebuild them once (`db/schema/table-rebuild.ts`: every row and rowid kept, the
  table, index and triggers made from the same statements a new database runs, so both end the same; a later start
  rebuilds nothing; foreign keys off for the copy, so a row whose parent is gone never stops the start — it is kept and
  counted in a log line, `foreign_key_check`). An older build reading a `refused` write answers a report for it as a conflict (409), never moves it.
- **The person's answer about a write in doubt** (「済んでいた」／「されていなかった」, the write card's existing buttons for a
  `bf_` card): `POST /browser-frame-runs/:runId/confirm { write, answer: done | not_done }` (exactly these keys) with this
  PC's key — **bound like a sure report**: the member's own run (anyone else's, another company's: the same 404) and
  **the run's own PC** (`BROWSER_DEVICE_ELSEWHERE`). Only a write that is `unknown` (`BROWSER_FRAME_NOT_UNKNOWN`), only
  **past its permit + 5 s + 30 s on both clocks** (`BROWSER_TOO_EARLY`: it may still be on its way), **once** (another
  answer after it: `BROWSER_FRAME_CONFIRMED`; the same answer again — the first one's reply lost — is answered as kept,
  nothing written, so the card's second press settles it; Codex review). A write the app gave up while on its way (the
  run's wait ran out, the DevTools connection went) keeps its permit for this (`Held.write`; Codex review). Kept in `browser_frame_write_confirms` (additive: the answer, **who** and **when**; never
  changed or removed while its run exists, by trigger). **It never moves the write or the run**: a locked run stays
  locked whatever the answer (simple: an answer is the person's word, not the site's), and no report changes the write
  once answered: one that agrees is `recorded`, one that contradicts it (`done` after 「されていなかった」, `refused` /
  `not_sent` after 「済んでいた」) is 409 `BROWSER_REPORT_CONFLICT` and noted in a log line (run id, write number, the two
  words; no value). After 「されていなかった」 the person asks again in the chat — a new run; nothing is sent again
  by itself. The run's view carries `confirmableAt` and `confirmed` per write.
- **The AI never reaches it**: no AI tool, no AI-side IPC. Only the card's buttons → `browserLines:confirmWrite` (main
  window only, the IPC that already served the line path's answer) → the work wiring's `confirm` (main's memory: the write
  in doubt and its time) → `frameConfirm` with this PC's key (`client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` checks which
  files name them). **The card follows the server**: when the work ends, and after any press the server did not take,
  main reads the run again (`GET /browser-frame-runs/:runId`) and takes the server's `confirmableAt` (the buttons' time),
  an answer it already holds (`confirmed`) or the site's late answer (the write's state); `BROWSER_FRAME_CONFIRMED` /
  `BROWSER_FRAME_NOT_UNKNOWN` with nothing more to read stop the buttons — never a loop of 「もう一度押してください」. The server
  decides at the press.
- **The model** (`FramesLife.tla`, README): `refused` (the run goes on, its unit kept: `BrokenFramesRefusedGivesUnitBack`
  → R14), `PersonConfirms` (R5 `R5_NotDoneStaysTrue`: `BrokenFramesConfirmBeforeWindow`; never unlocking:
  `BrokenFramesConfirmUnlocks` → R10). 264,480 states.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` "recovering from a failed write" (a refused write never done, the run
open, the corrected try a new permit within the max; refused only from the run's PC, late after unknown keeps the lock;
the answer once, past the window, from the run's PC, the member's own, exact keys, who and when, never unlocking, no
report after it; only an unknown write; the tables' moves and the answer kept as given; a database from before rebuilt
once with its rows, ending like a new one), `client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (a 4xx `site-refused`: the
page gets its error, the run goes on, the max holds; only the listed 4xx refused — every other 4xx, 5xx and outside
200–499 unknown and locked;
the wiring: a refused save then a corrected one; the card's answer from its time, sent with that write, never resending;
only the card reaches it), `model/browser-plan/check`, permission matrix row + `CROSS`, `jobs-schema.test.ts`,
`company-scoped-sql.test.ts`. Each guard mutation-checked.

## The write ledger across requests (a write in doubt holds its target for every later run)

Codex's external review, owner approved: the once-rules above hold **inside one run** only. A run ends (its hour, `end`,
leaving), the next request is a new run, and "created, but the answer was lost" → "ask again tomorrow" put the same thing
on the site twice: the old lock (`locked`) ended with its run. The ledger carries the doubt across runs, by mechanism —
no word was added to the operator's prompt (owner: the AI is kept by mechanisms, not by sentences). No site word.

- **The ledger** (`browser_write_ledger`, `db/schema/browser-frames.ts`, additive; the handle's `browserFrames`
  `insertLedger` / `ledgerDoubts`): one row per **permitted** write, written in the permit's own transaction — company ×
  registered domain × **outside account** × **target** (`sha256:` of `browserWriteTarget`: the method, the body's
  `_method`, the path with its raw query — never kept as text; the frame's other fixed fields play no part), with its run,
  write and time. What became of the write is its row in `browser_frame_writes` / `browser_frame_write_confirms`: done,
  not sent, refused (known) or `permitted` / `unknown` with no answer (**a doubt**). Triggers: a row only beside its
  `permitted` write with its run's member and domain (`browser_write_ledger_beside_write`, made again by the writes
  table's rebuild, which renames that table), never updated, never removed while its run exists.
- **The outside account** (`ledgerAccountOf`): the core knows no site's account marker, and each member's window holds
  their own login (one partition per account × company × site), so the account is the member's login,
  `member:<user id>`; its own column, so a site's reviewed account reader can name one later without changing the table.
  Decision: two members sharing one outside login are two accounts here (each is held by their own doubts only).
- **The guard** (`permit`, after the frame match, before the gate): a held write whose target has a doubt of this account
  on this domain — **any run, ended or not, however old** — → 409 `BROWSER_WRITE_IN_DOUBT`, nothing taken, the run goes
  on (another target is not held up). **No time limit**: neither the run's hour nor any clock lifts it.
- **Since "Sent is not done"** a `refused` write nobody read again and found unchanged is a doubt too (not for its own run,
  and not once a later write of that run to the same target was `done`).
- **What ends a doubt**: (a) the person's answer, 「済んでいた」 or 「されていなかった」 — the existing `confirm` of that
  earlier run ("Recovering from a failed write": past its window, the run's own PC, once); (b) the site's answer, late
  (`report` `unknown` → `done` / `refused`, as before) or **read back by the app** (below). Leaving the company keeps it:
  the write on its way becomes `unknown` (`memberLeft`) and stays in doubt for that member's account; nobody else's runs
  are held up, and a member invited back is asked first.
- **Asked before the operator works** (`start` answers `doubts`: the doubts whose frame `browserFramesMayMeet` one of the
  new run's frames — the same method and `_method`, the query, segment by segment with placeholders — wider than the
  permit's exact target on purpose; the earlier run and write, the frame, its times). The app (`work.ts` `askEarlier`)
  shows them on this run's card one by one, before the preview of a Skill run or the plan card of an ad-hoc one, before
  any operator step: headline `earlierDoubt` 「前回の結果が分からない書き込みがあります。…」, the frame line, and the card's
  existing ［済んでいた］／［されていなかった］ (`browserLines:confirmWrite`; the entry's `inDoubt.runId` names the earlier run, so
  `frameConfirm` / `frameGet` go to it). Every one answered 「されていなかった」 (or the site's answer found meanwhile) → the
  work goes on as before. 「済んでいた」 → the request stops, nothing written (`earlier-done`: this request may be that same
  write again — the consultation AI hears it and asks the person); no answer within the card's time (10 / 21 minutes from
  the start) → `earlier-unknown`; the turn stopped → its own reason. A run whose frames meet no doubt asks nothing
  (作業完遂性: only a write whose result nobody knows is asked about).
- **The read-back** (`work.ts` `readBack`, `readBackLanded` in `client/shared/browser-frame-writes.ts`), so the person's
  buttons are left for what is truly unknown: after the work, the write in doubt (the one with a permit) is read again at
  **its very address** — only when a Skill's `after_read` names it, it was read right before that write, and no other
  write of the run went there — and counts as landed only when **every** field it sent now reads exactly the sent value
  **and** at least one read otherwise before (a state already so proves nothing), compared on the **whole** JSON answers,
  each field a top-level plain value present on both sides (never the card's shortened rows: Codex review), and only for a
  write whose body was read whole (`sentBodyWhole`: no repeated or nested field the comparison would miss). Then the app reports `done` with reason
  `read-back` (`BROWSER_REPORT_REASONS`; a sure report: the run's own PC only) and, once the server took it, the card says
  `readBackDone` and the AI hears `read-back` 「…読み直したところ、その書き込みは済んでいました…」. A create whose answer
  was lost has no address to read (`{created}` is unknown), so it is always asked: that is the duplicate this ledger
  exists for.
- **The model**: `model/browser-plan/LedgerLife.tla` (README "LedgerLife"; R17, R18; `BrokenLedgerDoubtExpires` → R17,
  `BrokenLedgerReadBackGuess` → R18). FramesLife is unchanged (inside a run nothing moved).
- **Rolling back**: the table is additive. An older server neither writes nor reads it, so the guard is off there (as
  today); rolled forward again, its rows are as they were and every doubt in them holds. An older app ignores `doubts`
  (the permit still refuses the target, the write is not sent and the operator sees it as a write with no permit).
- **Remaining limits** (said): the target is the exact address — a site that names one object by two addresses (a
  trailing slash, another case, an alias, `05` for `5`) is two targets to the guard, though the start's question compares
  patterns; the read-back proves the state, not its cause (someone else setting the very same values in between counts);
  only the frames path keeps the ledger (the old one-write plans and the opened lines have their own target locks and
  confirms, and neither reads it);
  two members sharing one outside login are two accounts (above); a person who answers wrongly is believed (as for the
  earlier card); the answer stays bound to the earlier run's PC ("Recovering from a failed write"), so a member who moved
  to another PC cannot answer there (the guard then holds that target for them until they answer on that PC — the safe
  side; an owner / admin path is not built).

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` "the write ledger across requests" (days and a month later still
refused — never by time —, another target goes on, a placeholder target: the same object held, another not, and the
start names a frame that may meet; the person's answer either way, a late done / read-back / late refusal end it, a
read-back from another PC ends nothing, another member and another company never mixed in, a member who left, the
table's triggers and no path kept), `browser-frame-match.test.ts` (`browserWriteTarget`, `browserFramesMayMeet` both ways),
`client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (asked on the card before any operator step and answered on the earlier run,
「済んでいた」 stops with nothing written, no answer in time / an answer held elsewhere / the turn stopped, two doubts in turn,
none asked when the start names none, the read-back found landed — and not when the server did not take it, the body was
not read whole, nothing changed or the value differs —, `readBackLanded` on whole answers (a cut value, a field absent
before), `sentBodyWhole`), the writes table's rebuild re-making the ledger's trigger,
`model/browser-plan/check`, `jobs-schema.test.ts`, `company-scoped-sql.test.ts`. Each guard mutation-checked.

## A card in its consultation's own window (［任せる］／［やめる］／［済んでいた］ from 「別ウィンドウで開く」)

A consultation opened in its own window (`chat-and-agents.md`, `features/chat-windows`) shows the write cards its AI made,
so their buttons work there too — and only there and in the main window.

- **The one rule** (`features/chat-windows/ipc.ts` `showsConsultation(event, companyId, sessionId)`, the shell's window
  registry): the event comes from the **main frame** of the app's main window, or of the window the app opened for **that
  very consultation** of that company (its registry entry: `sessionId` and `companyId` equal). Everything else is refused:
  a window of another consultation, an unknown sender (a preview page, the browser operation's window — no preload —, a
  document view), any frame inside a window (a plugin's frame, a document's). A card with no consultation (null): the main
  window only. A window that closed no longer counts.
- **Injected as the registry itself** (`main.ts`: `cardWindows: chatWindows`, the very `ChatWindows` it made — a stand-in
  would have to be a whole registry, by type) into the write cards' IPC (`write-lines.ts`), whose one `fromCardWindow`
  asks it, for every read and press of a card — the line path's `bl_` write card (its entry's `conversationId`) and a
  write run's `bf_` card (`frameCards.placeOf`, the work wiring's entry: the consultation `browser_work` ran in) —, with
  **that card's own company and consultation**; no BrowserWindow check anywhere else. A card the member does not have
  answers `gone` from any window. Tested with the real registry (independent review of #860).
- **One press wins** when the card shows in two windows (the main window's inbox and the consultation's window): a press
  while another is on its way (`Entry.pressing`: a `bf_` card's ［任せる］ and answer, a `bl_` card's answer) asks the server
  nothing and returns the card as it stands **once that press is through** (Codex review: it waits for it, so it never
  shows buttons already answered); the card moves on by itself in both — read again every 2 s while a `bf_` run goes on,
  and while answer buttons are up, also once they can be pressed, **backing off** (the polling-reduction plan;
  `writeCardRefreshMs` / `WRITE_CARD_UNSETTLED_REFRESH`, `src/features/browser-plans/model.ts`): every 15 s for the first
  2 minutes, every 60 s up to 10 minutes in all, then not at all until the card is shown again or its window comes to the
  front (a `bl_` card's read asks the server). A later press finds the card no longer `ready` (as before).
- **Unchanged**: the one-time approval value stays in the work wiring (main's memory); the AI's tools reach none of this;
  the read-only inspection's card (`browserLines:card`) stays main-window only.

Tests: `client/tests/chat-windows.test.ts` (the rule: the main window, that consultation's window, not another's, not
another company's, not with no consultation, not an unknown sender, not a frame inside, not once closed),
`browser-write-lines-app.test.ts` (with the real registry: a `bl_` card from its consultation's window, never another's
or a frame inside its own; a `bf_` card's read, ［任せる］, ［やめる］ and answer only from a window showing its consultation,
never a frame inside it, `gone` without a card; a `bl_` card answered from two windows at once: one answer asked),
`browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (the card in two windows: one ［任せる］ and one answer asked of the server, the other
window shown the outcome; a lost reply frees the next press), `src/features/browser-plans/model.test.ts` (when a card
reads itself again, and the back-off). Each guard mutation-checked.

## Writes only to what this run made (`{made:<id>}` in a Skill's `browser.yaml`)

A company Skill's declaration can say "this write may only target something this same run created": make a plan, then add
items **to that plan only**, never to one the person (or an earlier run) made. A generic core rule (no site word in the core).

```yaml
writes:
  - { id: new-plan, method: POST, path: /api/plans, max: 1 }
  - { method: POST, path: "/api/plans/{made:new-plan}/items", max: 10 }   # quoted in a flow mapping ({ … })
```

- **The grammar** (`shared/core/browser-skill.ts`): a write target may carry `id` (`BROWSER_SKILL_WRITE_ID_RE`: a lower-case
  letter, then lower-case letters, digits, `-`; ≤ 32; once per declaration, else `bad-write-id`); a write path may hold
  **one** `{made:<id>}` segment naming an **earlier** target's id — an undeclared id, a later target, the target itself or
  two such segments → `bad-made` (refused at parse, so 「Skill の承認」 and a run's start refuse it too). **Only in a
  reviewed company Skill**: an ad-hoc plan (the AI wrote it) carrying `{made}` is refused — by the app before asking
  (`parseWorkAnswer`, `plan-writes`) and by the server at its start (`browserAdhocWritesProblem` → `made`,
  `BROWSER_FRAME_BAD_PLAN`; Codex review: the parsed place is no wire format). **Only a create names what it made**: an
  `id` only on a POST without `_method` (`bad-maker`: a PATCH of the person's object answering its id would otherwise
  hand that id on). **No broader target beside it**: another target of the same method whose pattern also fits the
  `{made}` target's addresses (`{x}`, or `{n}` / another `{made}` where it has a placeholder; fixed fields not looked at)
  → `made-covered`. Admins approving such a Skill check that the maker endpoint **mints** its ids (a create, not an
  upsert or an id the caller chooses). Never in `after_read` / `never_read` (`isBrowserSkillPath`'s `made` only for writes). The
  parsed path — and so the server's frames table, the plan hash and the card — carries the maker's **place** instead
  (`{made:2}` = the second target; `browserMadeFrom`), so no table changed; the parsed spelling cannot be written by hand
  (an id starts with a letter).
- **The match** (`shared/core/browser-frame-match.ts`, the one rule): `browserFrameMismatch(frame, request, made?)` — a
  `{made:<n>}` segment fits only one of `made.get(n)`, the ids the n-th frame's own done writes returned **in this run**,
  and is still one plain segment (never a dot segment or an encoded `/`); without `made` nothing fits. A path that fits but
  for that id → the fixed reason `made` (`BROWSER_FRAME_MISMATCHES`), otherwise `path` as before.
- **The server decides** (`services/browser-frames.ts`): the id comes with the maker write's **`done` report**
  (`{ write, result: done, created }`) — like every sure report only from **the run's own PC** —, only while that write
  moves `permitted` → `done`, only a plain token (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`); anything else 400 and nothing moves. **Since "The
  run's promise"** an id of any frame's done write is kept (what "a target this run made" is read from); a `{made:<n>}` frame
  still fits only the n-th frame's own. It is kept in **`browser_frame_made`** (additive, append-only: no update,
  no delete while its run exists; a trigger accepts a row only beside a `done` write of that very frame; a CHECK on the
  token) — **the only value of a site's page the server keeps**. Every permit reads that run's rows; another run never
  inherits them. A repeated `done` report is `same` only with the same id (another id or none → `BROWSER_REPORT_CONFLICT`).
  **No id**: an `unknown`, `refused` or `not_sent` write gives none; a late `done` after `unknown` records none (the run is
  locked anyway); a refused try whose corrected try is `done` gives that one's id. `max` still applies to the `{made}` frame.
- **The app** (`features/browser/frame-writes.ts`): a done write of a frame a later `{made:<n>}` names reports the id its
  answer names (`createdIdOf`: `id`, else `data.id`, the same reading as an after-read `{created}`); once the server
  recorded the report, the id is kept in main's memory and `pickFrame` matches later held writes against it with the same
  core rule (so a write to another object is refused before any permit is asked). An id the held write itself carried (a
  body field's value or a segment of its path: an upsert, an id the caller chose) is **never reported** (independent
  review). No id in the answer → none reported, the
  `{made}` frame stays shut for the run (availability, never a wider write). A report the server did not record locks the
  run as before.
- **The card** (the member's plan card and the admin's 「Skill の承認」, `browserSkillWriteLine`): 「…：10回まで（この作業の中で、
  1つ目の書き込み先で作ったものにだけ）」.
- **Rolling back**: an older server refuses a declaration with `id` (`bad-writes`), so no run with `{made}` frames exists
  there, and an older app never sends `created`.
- **The model** (`model/browser-plan/README.md` "FramesLife"): not modelled — which frame a held write fits is a
  precondition of `AppAsks` there (matching is the core rule's, tested here); `{made}` only narrows which writes fit, so
  every rule of FramesLife holds as it is, and the model stays under its state ceiling.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-skill-core.test.ts` (an id names a target, the place in the parsed path, block and flow
style; undeclared / later / itself / two segments / a bad or repeated id / the parsed spelling written by hand refused;
an id on a PATCH or an override refused; a broader target of the same method beside a `{made}` one refused;
not in `after_read` / `never_read`; the card's words), `browser-frame-match.test.ts` (an ad-hoc plan's `{made}` refused;
nothing fits without what was made,
another frame's or another id; the rest of the path first; a made id one plain segment), `browser-frames.test.ts` (an
ad-hoc plan with `{made}` starts nothing; the frames' paths; nothing fits before the maker's done with an id, only that id after, `max`; an id only with `done`, only a
plain token, only for a maker; unknown / late done / refused give none, the corrected try does; another run inherits
nothing; another PC records nothing; the same done with another id a conflict; the table's trigger, CHECK, no update /
delete; a database from before rebuilt with the trigger as a new one has it), `client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts`
(an AI's plan with `{made}` refused; the maker's id with its done report, an id the request carried never reported, only that id written afterwards, none read → none written, a report not recorded →
locked and nothing kept, a failed maker write gives none). Each guard mutation-checked; kept on purpose: keeping the id in
the app only once the server recorded it (a report the server did not record locks the run, so nothing more is sent).

## A write with an exact query (`query` on a write of a Skill's `browser.yaml`)

A real site creates an object with `POST /api/…?return` (the query says what to answer). Before this, any held write whose
address carried a query fit no frame, so a company Skill could not permit it. A write target may now declare the **exact**
query its address must carry; everything else about frames is unchanged. A generic core rule (no site word in the core).

```yaml
writes:
  - { method: POST, path: /api/labels, query: "return", max: 1 }   # only POST /api/labels?return
  - { method: POST, path: /api/labels, max: 1 }                    # only POST /api/labels (no query at all)
```

- **The shape: one string, the raw query without `?`** (decision: not a map). The string is exactly what the address
  carries, so the match is a byte comparison and the card shows what is sent; a YAML map would lose the order, could not
  tell a bare `key` from `key=`, and would read `1` as a number. **The grammar** (`browserSkillQueryProblem`,
  `shared/core/browser-skill.ts`, the one canonical spelling): 1–5 parts joined by `&` (≤ 100 characters,
  `BROWSER_SKILL_LIMITS.writeQuery`); a part is `key` or `key=value`, each a plain word (`A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ ~ -`: no `%`,
  `+`, `;`, `#`, `?`, space, no empty part), so no character has two spellings; **`key=` (an empty value) is refused** — a
  bare `key` is the only way to say "present without a value"; a key once (A–Z folded); **no key containing `method`**
  (A–Z folded: `_method`, `X-HTTP-Method-Override`, `_HttpMethod`… — an override never rides in the address, I4) and no
  login / CSRF / session name (`isSensitiveFieldName`). Anything else → `bad-query`. A value is a fixed word or — since
  "A query value not fixed" below — exactly `{n}` / `{x}`; a name never holds a placeholder. The path itself still never
  holds a `?` (`bad-path`).
- **The parsed write** (`BrowserSkillWrite.query: string | null`, **required in the type**, so no copy of a frame can drop
  it — a frame that lost its query would let the same path through without one). The same path with and without a query,
  or with two queries, are separate targets (the duplicate check includes it). `{n}`, `{x}`, `{made:<id>}` and every path
  rule are unchanged; `made-covered` still compares paths only (overlap is enough, the safe side).
- **The match** (`browserFrameMismatch`, the one rule the server's permit and the app ask): the request's `path` is the path
  and — only when the address has one — `?` + its raw query exactly as sent (the permit's wire shape is unchanged: a query
  used to make it fit nothing). Split at the first `?`; the path checked as before (`path` / `made`); then **a frame without
  a query fits only an address with no `?` at all** (also `/a?`), **a frame with one only the identical raw string** —
  no decoding or reordering, so another order, `%72`, `;`, `+`, a fragment, a repeated or extra key, `key=` for `key` are
  each just another string → reason **`query`**; a frame whose query is not the grammar's (not from a parsed declaration)
  fits nothing. The body's `_method` and the fixed fields are asked after it, as before.
- **Only a reviewed company Skill**: an ad-hoc plan (the AI wrote it) with a query is refused — by the app's
  `parseWorkAnswer` (`plan-writes`) and the server's start (`browserAdhocWritesProblem` → `query`,
  `BROWSER_FRAME_BAD_PLAN`), like `{made}` (decision, safe side: a query can change what a write does and only the card
  would review it).
- **Approval** — the Skill's approval key is the hash of `browser.yaml`, so another query is another declaration: it needs
  approving again, and a run on the old one ends at its next permit (`BROWSER_SKILL_NOT_APPROVED`). The run's `planHash`
  also covers each frame's query (only when it has one, so a plan without queries hashes as before; a second layer behind
  the declaration hash, which already moves).
- **Stored** (`browser_frames.query`, `migrateBrowserFrameQuery`: an additive nullable column, idempotent; **a row from
  before reads null = no query**, what it meant then; a CHECK repeats the alphabet, 1–100 characters; trigger
  `browser_frames_fixed_query` keeps it fixed). Answered in the run's view as `BrowserFrameView.query`, **absent when
  none** (an older app ignores it: it refuses every address with a query by itself, and the server refuses one without, so
  a query frame is simply unusable there; its card would show the path alone, for a write it can never send).
- **The app** (`client/shared/browser-frame-writes.ts`): `workFrameOf` keeps the view's query (`WorkFrame.query` required,
  null for none); `pickFrame` asks the core with it; the permit carries `path?query` as read from the held URL
  (`readHeldWrite`: the WHATWG URL's `search`, so the bytes compared are the ones the write session sends; a bare `?`, which
  `search` drops, is kept as an empty query — `HeldWriteShape.query` null only when the address has no `?` — so no frame
  fits it, independent review). The operator's frames list it. An id the held write's query carried is never reported as
  made (`sentCarries`). An ad-hoc plan's writes go to the start **without** a `query` key (`AdhocPlanWrite`: the server's
  strict parser refuses even `query: null`; Codex review), tested by a round trip through the server's parser.
- **The card** (`browserSkillWriteLine`, the member's plan card and the admin's 「Skill の承認」):
  「/api/labels?return（アドレスの ? のあとが「return」とまったく同じときだけ） に送る（作る・保存する）：1回まで」; the result
  card's line is `POST /api/labels?return`.
- **The model** (`model/browser-plan/README.md` "FramesLife"): unchanged — which frame a held write fits is a precondition
  of `AppAsks`, and a query only narrows which writes fit (as `{made}`); no state or transition is added.
- **Rolling back**: an older server refuses a declaration with `query` (`bad-writes`) and has no column use (the column
  stays, nullable); an older app never fits a query frame (above).

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-skill-core.test.ts` (the grammar as an accept / refuse table — empty, six parts, an empty
part, `key=`, `=v`, `a=1=2`, a key twice or in another case, `%`, `+`, space, `;`, `#`, `?`, `/`, non-ASCII, every
method-override spelling, login / CSRF / session names, over 100; a number or a map; a `?` in the path; kept as written,
null when absent, two targets by query, the frames' copy; the plain words), `browser-frame-match.test.ts` (a frame without
a query refuses any, `?` alone too; the exact string and every near miss; the declared order only; the path and `{made}`
first; an override still asked in the body; a frame holding a query the grammar refuses fits nothing; an ad-hoc plan),
`browser-frames.test.ts` (the view carries it, absent for none; the permit takes exactly it and refuses each near miss,
taking no unit; another query needs approving again and ends a run on the old one; an ad-hoc plan with a query refused;
the column's trigger and CHECK; a database from before the column: its row reads null and keeps its meaning),
`client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (the view's query kept, absent = null; exact fit only; the permit's path; the
card and result lines; an id the query carried never reported). Each guard mutation-checked; kept on purpose: the plan
hash's query (the declaration hash already moves with it).

### A query value not fixed (`key={x}` / `key={n}`; production incident 2026-11-27)

A company Skill declared `{ method: POST, path: /api/tags, query: "return", max: 1 }`; on the real site ［作成］ sends
`POST /api/tags?return=<a value>`. The format-2 recording said `bareQuery: false`, but a recording keeps no value, so
nobody could write the exact query: the held write was refused as outside the frame (nothing sent) and the run stopped,
saying 「画面が先に進めませんでした」. Generic (no site word in the core):

- **The grammar** (`browserSkillQueryProblem`): a part's **value** may be exactly `{n}` (1–64 ASCII digits) or `{x}` (1–64
  of `A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ ~ -`), the whole value (`return={x}x`, `{X}`, `{made:1}`, `{k}` refused). A **name** never is
  (`{x}`, `{x}=1` refused) and stays exact; every other rule holds: 1–5 parts, a name once, no override / login / CSRF /
  session name, ≤ 100 characters.
- **The match** (`browserSkillQueryFits`, asked by `browserFrameMismatch` — the server's permit and the app's pre-check
  alike): a declaration without a placeholder is still the identical string; with one, the same number of `&` parts in the
  declared order, each name byte for byte, an `=` exactly where declared, a literal value byte for byte and a placeholder
  value of its kind — compared part by part even when the strings are equal (a sent `?page={n}` is no number: Codex
  review). So an empty value (`?return=`), a bare name (`?return`), an encoded value (`%41`), `+`, `;`, `#`,
  a second `=`, `/`, non-ASCII, over 64, a missing / extra / repeated / reordered name and `_method` never fit (`query`).
- **Stored** (`browser_frames.query`): its CHECK (added in place, unchangeable without a rebuild) allows only the plain
  alphabet, so the adapter keeps a placeholder value as `==n` / `==x` (`storedFrameQuery` / `frameQueryFromStored`,
  `adapters/sqlite/browser-frames.ts`) — a spelling the grammar never makes (a value never begins with `=`). A server
  rolled back reads `return==x`, which its grammar refuses: that frame fits nothing (the safe side).
- **Approval**: `return` → `return={x}` is another `browser.yaml`, so another declaration hash: approved anew, and a run on
  the old one ends at its next permit (tested).
- **The card** (`browserSkillWriteLine`, `browserSkillQueryWords`): 「/api/tags?return=（何かの値）（アドレスの ? のあとが
  「return=（何かの値）」の形のときだけ。（何かの…）のところの値は決まっていません） に送る…」 (`{n}` → （何かの数字）); the
  result card's line `POST /api/tags?return=（何かの値）` — never the value sent.
- **The draft** ("A Skill's draft from a recording"): a recorded write with `bareQuery: false` is no longer left out — each
  kept name becomes `name={x}` (`?kind&return` → `kind={x}&return={x}`), marked `query-value-open` (the words say the value
  is not kept and fits any; fix it to the fixed value when there is one); a name whose last word ends in `id` with its
  value open (`?item_id={x}`) is also marked `updates-existing` — it may point at any existing thing (independent review). D8 holds: no value is stored, only placeholders. A
  masked name (`{x}` in the kept query) is still left out (`query`); a format-1 recording still cannot say how its query was
  sent (`query-values`).
- **Why the run stopped** (`workEndAfterRefusals`, `client/shared/browser-frame-writes.ts`, applied in `work.ts`): when a
  held write was refused as outside the frames (`refused`) and the operator then stopped on `blocked`, `need-person` or
  `not-found`, the reason is `refused` — the AI's fixed sentence and the card's headline say
  「止まりました：書き込みが承認した範囲に合わなかったため、送らずに止めました。」. The app's own stops (a button, the time, the
  network, the login…), `done` and `unknown` are kept.
- **The refused write's line** (`workRefusedWriteLine`): its address in the recording's kept form (`browserRunAddressPattern`:
  digits `{n}`, other words `{x}`, query names only) with `name=…` where the name carried a value —
  `POST /api/tags?return=…（枠の外）`; another host or an unreadable address: `POST （枠の外）` as before. The member's card
  only, as every write line.
- **Rolling back the app**: an older app's core treats `return={x}` as a literal string, so it never fits a real request
  (refused, nothing sent); deploy the server first, as for every frame rule.
- **The model** (FramesLife): unchanged — a placeholder only changes which writes fit a frame, a precondition of `AppAsks`.

Tests: `browser-skill-core.test.ts` (accept / refuse table: values, a placeholder as a name, in part of a value, other
placeholders, a name twice, override / login names; the card's words), `browser-frame-match.test.ts` (any one plain value;
empty, bare, encoded, `+`, `;`, `#`, `/`, non-ASCII, `=`, over 64, extra / missing / repeated / reordered names, another
case; `{n}` digits only; two names with a literal beside; a placeholder as a name fits nothing), `browser-frames.test.ts`
(the permit with a value, every near miss taking no unit, the stored spelling; `return` → `return={x}` needs approving
again and ends the old run), `browser-skill-draft.test.ts` (`bareQuery` false → `{x}` per name, marked, the frame fitting
the real request; a masked name left out; the generated recordings), `client/tests/browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (the app
fits and permits `?return=<value>`, the card's （何かの値）; the refused line's kept form; the stop reason as a table and the
incident end to end). Each guard mutation-checked.

## A Skill's draft from a recording (PRs 1–2 of 4: the core's two rules; the draft kept for its author's AI)

Owner-approved flow 2026-11-27 (internal design memo 「記録からSkillの下書き_設計メモ」): a member records the work once with
test material and sends it; a **draft** company Skill is made from it — `browser.yaml` by the core's deterministic rule below,
`SKILL.md` by the member's own AI —, only its author may try it (the card every run), and an owner / admin approves it into a
department's `.claude/skills/<name>/`. PR 1 is the rule; PR 2 (below) keeps the draft on the server and lets the author's
own AI read it with the author's own recordings and write its `SKILL.md`; the trial run (PR 3) and the request (PR 4) are
later.

- **`browserSkillDraftFrom(recordings)`** (`shared/core/browser-skill-draft.ts`, pure, knows no site): 1–5 recordings of one
  domain, each one the server's rule accepts (`browserRecordingProblem`), in the order made. Only sends with POST / PUT /
  PATCH that the site answered **2xx** become write targets (a 3xx may be an expired session sent to the login page, having
  done nothing — independent review); left out, each with a fixed reason
  (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_WORDS.leftOut`): no answer, not taken, **the method DELETE or `_method=DELETE`** (`deletes`), a path
  beginning with a placeholder or `/` alone (`wide-path`: the ad-hoc plan's rule), **a `{x}` anywhere that did not become
  `{made}`** (`any-segment`: the recording masks `v2`, `save.php`, camelCase and every word the person hid to `{x}`, which
  would fit any segment — independent review), a path the declaration cannot spell, a
  query whose names are placeholders or name a method / login field (`query`), **a query the recording does not vouch for as
  sent exactly** (`query-values`: format 1, or `bareQuery` false — the kept form drops values, so `?preview=false` kept as
  `?preview` would otherwise permit a request never sent; Codex review of the memo), an override the declaration cannot say
  (`override`). `max` is the most a target was sent **in one recording** (what one run did; never the sum over recordings,
  independent review), ≤ 20. `bareQuery` is the app's word, as the whole recording is (made by its window from the person's
  actions with this PC's key; an AI can neither make nor send one): a draft stays its author's until an owner / admin
  approves it. `_method` PUT / PATCH
  becomes the fixed field. **`{made:<id>}`** replaces a path's one placeholder when exactly one earlier create of the same
  recording (a POST without override whose path holds no placeholder and whose answer carried `id` / `data.id`, the ids the
  app reports) is there; never across recordings, never with two candidates, never a later create; beside a broader target of
  the same method (`made-covered`) every link is dropped. It then fits only an id the site handed this run — whether the
  endpoint mints its ids stays the reviewer's check at 「Skill の承認」, as for every `{made}`. **Marks** for the author and the
  reviewer (fixed words, `BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_WORDS.marks`): `updates-existing` (PUT / PATCH / an override, or a placeholder
  left unlinked), `id-in-body` (a create's body field whose last word ends in `id`, typed rather than taken from an earlier
  answer and not empty, with the fields named: fixing its new-object value keeps the write from changing an existing one —
  the hand-made Skill's `aid: "0"`), `may-delete` (a path segment or query name saying delete / remove / destroy / trash /
  erase / purge / discard: a POST can delete too, so only the method DELETE is left out). The text has one spelling (flow
  mappings, quoted strings, ids `w<n>`), the same recordings always give the same text, and the rule parses its own text
  before answering (none rather than a guess). No draft: nothing, mixed domains, more than 5 recordings, no target, more than
  16 targets.
- **`browserSkillDraftCoveredProblem(draft, base)`**: a later change of a draft's declaration is taken only when it is no
  wider than `base` (what the recordings gave): the same domain; each write covered by a base write — the same method, query
  and `_method`, every fixed field of the base kept (more may be added), a path of the same length whose every segment is the
  same, or narrower (`{x}` → a literal, `{n}` or `{made}`; `{n}` → digits or `{made}`; `{made}` → `{made}`), and **a draft
  `{made:<k>}` still made by a write within the base's own maker** for that segment, or within a plain create of the base (a
  POST without `_method` and without a placeholder) for a base `{n}` / `{x}` — never re-pointed at a write that updates an
  existing thing and answers its id (independent review) —; **the uses of
  the draft writes one base write covers, together, at most 20** (`too-many-uses`: one target split into many literal ones keeps
  one budget; Codex review); never a delete, never a path beginning with a placeholder. `never_read`, `after_read` and the
  account marker may be added (they only narrow). `max` may exceed what was recorded, up to 20: an ad-hoc plan's card allows
  the same.
- **Recordings of format 2** ("Recording" above): every send carries `bareQuery`; the server takes format 1 and 2, the app
  writes 2. **Deploy the server before handing out the app**: an older server refuses a format-2 recording (`format`), and the
  app keeps it for another try.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-skill-draft.test.ts` (the two Lステップ recordings' shapes — the tag's create with
`?return`, the action's register, the route's create, the save of an existing route marked `updates-existing`, `aid` marked
`id-in-body` — give exactly the expected text, and the hand-made `lstep-round-inflow` declaration is a change within it;
every reason left out; a query only when vouched for (format 1 and `?preview=false` never); the server's check of format 2;
`may-delete`; a 3xx not taken; a `{x}` left out unless it became `{made}`; max = the most in one recording; `{made}` only for one earlier create of the same recording, `data.id`, never a guess,
dropped beside a broader target, the frame then fitting only what was made; 300 generated recordings: the text parses, is
within itself, never deletes, each write one the person sent; the change rule: drops, reorders, narrowing, fixed fields, the
shared budget, a `{made}` never re-pointed at an update, every refusal), `client/tests/browser-recorder.test.ts` (`bareQuery` for each kind of query; format 2 passes the
server's check). Each guard mutation-checked (25 mutations; a malformed address beside `bareQuery` is refused, never thrown — Codex review).

### PR 2: the draft stored, read and changed by its author's own AI

The server keeps a draft per recording it can make one from; the member's own consultation AI reads it — and only the
member's own recordings — through a new tool, asks the member 2–4 questions and writes the `SKILL.md`. Nothing runs a draft
yet (PR 3) and nothing requests it for the company (PR 4).

- **Made when a recording is sent** (`services/browser-recordings.ts` `send` → `services/browser-skill-drafts.ts`
  `makeOnSend`, in the recording's transaction, **in a savepoint**: a failure while making the draft keeps the recording
  without a draft — logged, `draftNote` says it can be made again —, never a draft without its recording): when `browserSkillDraftFrom` gives a
  draft and the sender has room, a row with the rule's text as `base_declaration` and `declaration`, empty `steps`, the
  name `draft-<6 hex>`. The answer (`BrowserRecordingSent`, additive: an older app reads the summary only) adds `draftId`
  and `draftNote` (plain Japanese: made, or why not — no write the rule keeps, or 10 drafts already). The recording is kept
  either way (it is the company's too: its admins read it). The app's sentence after ［送る］ (`BROWSER_RECORDING_MAIN_WORDS`
  `sentWithDraft` 「…Skill の下書きができました。相談に戻って、AI に「記録を送った」と伝えてください。」, `sentWithoutDraft(note)`
  — the old `sent` sentence (the admins' path) then the reason;
  an older server's answer: `sent` as before), and the review screen's note says the member's own consultation AI reads it
  to make a draft (other people's AI does not).
- **Table** `browser_skill_drafts` (`db/schema/browser-skill-drafts.ts`, **new table only**, behind the handle's
  `browserSkillDrafts`): company, `bd_` + 32 hex, author (`user_id`), `name` (the Skill name rule, ≤ 64; once among the author's drafts they can see — checked by the service in its transaction, no unique index: a draft past `delete_at` or from before a rejoin holds no name, Codex review),
  `domain`, `recording_ids` (JSON, the names stay after the recordings' 30 days), `base_declaration` and `base_notes` (each
  write's marks and id fields, what was left out — what the rule said, kept for the card and the request later),
  `declaration`, `steps` (`SKILL.md`, ≤ 32 KiB, CHECK), `version` (`sha256:` of the canonical JSON of `{ name,
  declaration, steps }`), `tried_version` (null until PR 3), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `delete_at`. A trigger keeps the
  author, domain, recordings, base and creation fixed. **Every repository method takes the author** (no method reads or
  changes another person's draft). **Not in a company archive** (`ARCHIVE_EXCLUDED_TABLES`).
- **Whose** (D9, I5): the author alone, while a member this time (`created_at` ≥ their membership's). Another member, an
  owner, an admin, another company's id, a draft past `delete_at`, the author after leaving and coming back: the same 404
  `BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_NOT_FOUND`, and nothing of it listed. 「Skill の承認」, the Skills' name resolution and
  `approvedFrames` never read this table (a draft is not a company Skill until PR 4 places it).
- **What the AI may read** (I1, D1 — the change of "Recording"'s rule that no AI reads a recording): through these
  routes only, the author's own recordings — sent while a member this time, still kept — in their stored kept form (no
  value). `GET /browser-skill-drafts` lists the author's own recordings (an owner's too: never the company's, which the
  admin console's `GET /browser-recordings` shows them); a draft's `sources` are the author's own of its `recording_ids`;
  `POST /browser-skill-drafts` takes only the author's own (anyone else's is the same 404 as none,
  `BROWSER_RECORDING_NOT_FOUND`). No record token, and no AI tool calls the recordings' own routes.
- **Routes** (`routes/browser-skill-drafts.ts`, `companyMember`, the session only; shapes `shared/types/browser-skill-drafts.ts`):
  `GET /browser-skill-drafts` (`{ drafts, recordings }`), `POST /browser-skill-drafts { recordingIds (1–5, distinct, in the
  order given, one domain), name?, replaces? }` (made again from own recordings: the work split over two recordings, as the
  Lステップ route's 「タグを作る」 and 「コピーする」; with `replaces` — one of the author's own drafts, anyone else's the same 404
  as none — that draft goes in the same step, does not count against the 10 and frees its name: remaking never fills the
  10, review of #889), `GET|PUT|DELETE /browser-skill-drafts/:draftId`. PUT `{ expectedVersion,
  name?, declaration?, steps? }` (exactly these keys, at least one change): the version must be the one read, else 409
  `BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_CHANGED` with the current `version` (the AI and a later screen changing it at once); a declaration
  must parse (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_BAD_DECLARATION`) and be **no wider than `base_declaration`**
  (`browserSkillDraftCoveredProblem`; `BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_WIDER` with the `problem` and the `write`: I2, I6); a name by the
  rule (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_BAD_NAME`), not another draft's of the author (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_NAME_TAKEN`); `steps` ≤ 32
  KiB (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_TOO_LARGE`, also the route's body limit). A change moves `delete_at` to 30 days on.
- **10 per person, 30 days** (D5): a create past 10 is 409 `BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_LIMIT`; a send past 10 keeps the
  recording without a draft and says so; both say the way out (「要らない下書きを消すよう AI に頼むか、30日たつと消えます。」: the
  tool's `delete`). Every read leaves out a row past `delete_at`; the server clock deletes them
  (`expireBrowserSkillDrafts`, `ClockRun.draftsDeleted`).
- **The tool** `browser_skill_draft` on `acb_browser` (`client/electron/browser-request/skill-draft-tool.ts`, words and checks
  `client/shared/browser-skill-drafts.ts`, the HTTP `client/electron/features/browser/skill-drafts.ts`): given **only beside
  `browser_work`** (its switch on, the wiring given: `browserInspectForTurn`; Claude Code only, never an unattended step),
  `action` `list` / `show` / `save` / `remake` (its `draft`: the draft it replaces) / `delete` (the AI confirms which with
  the member first). Main calls the drafts' routes with the member's session — never this PC's
  key, never `/browser-recordings` —, and checks a draft / recording id and the company id before any request (no path from
  the AI's words). `show` hands the draft (`browser.yaml`, the base when it differs, the marks' and left-out fixed words,
  `SKILL.md` **as written**: its 【】 are the member's) and then **every recording's lines as one block of outside text**
  (`browserRecordingLine` in `wrapOutsideText`: a label's 【】 → 〔〕, it cannot close the block). Answers are fixed words
  (`BROWSER_SKILL_DRAFT_AI_WORDS`) and the server's own plain sentences (≤ 200); 20 calls per turn. The description (plain
  Japanese, no 「開ける」) says how: read the member's own draft and recordings, ask 2–4 questions (what changes each time,
  what must not change, the test names), write `SKILL.md` with the standard sections the hand-made `lstep-round-inflow`
  taught — what it does, what to ask for, **the preview: read only, type nothing, press no saving button (named), no search
  box, browse by folder**, the steps, when to stop, what never to do, the test values — and only narrow `browser.yaml`.
  The tool row: 「Skill の下書きを読み書きしました」 (`acb-tool-displays.ts`).
- **The rollback floor for PR 3** (Codex point 8 on the memo): a frames run whose `source` this build does not know ends on
  its first touch when `open` or `waiting` (`services/browser-frames.ts` `settle`, `KNOWN_SOURCES` `skill` / `adhoc`, end
  reason `unknown-source`): a later build's `draft` run left behind is never permitted as an ad-hoc plan; a `locked` one (a
  write in doubt) keeps the domain to its hour, as for every source (review of #889). `deployment.md` "a Skill's draft and its
  trial runs".
- **Remaining limits** (said): `SKILL.md` goes back to the AI as written, so a label the AI copied into it from a recording
  is no longer marked as page text (it is the member's draft, read by the member's own AI); a label may name a customer the
  member saw (as before, hidden before sending is the member's choice); a modified app could forge a recording (out of
  scope, D10).

Tests (PR 2): `server/tests/unit/browser-skill-drafts.test.ts` (made with the recording, in a savepoint — a failure keeps
the recording without a draft, nothing of the draft half-written —, none from a read-only recording and why; a remake
replacing one of the author's own drafts — at 10, its name kept, anyone else's or a missing one 404, nothing replaced when
no draft can be made —; the limit's way out; the author alone — another member, an owner, an admin, another company,
the author after leaving and coming back: 404 and nothing listed; only own recordings listed, used and handed out — an
owner's create from a member's recording refused like none —; a recording past its 30 days no longer handed out; remake in
order, one domain, ≤ 5, distinct, strict body; narrowing taken, anything wider refused with its problem — a write never
recorded, a DELETE, a wide path, another domain, a target split past its budget —; the base and the author fixed by
trigger; the version's compare-and-set; the name rule and once per author, free again once its draft expired or after a rejoin; `SKILL.md` 32 KB; 10 per person; 30 days moved by
a change, left out and swept by the clock; deleting), `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` 「a run of a source this
build does not know」 (open and waiting end; locked keeps the domain), `client/tests/browser-skill-draft-tool.test.ts` (only the drafts' routes with the session and never
this PC's key, `delete` and a remake's `replaces` included; ids checked before any request; the recordings' lines one block of outside text, a label trying to close it
neutralized, `SKILL.md` as written; refusal words; the call budget; the tool only beside `browser_work`; the description),
`client/tests/browser-recording-app.test.ts` (the send's sentence with and without a draft, an older server), permission
matrix rows + `CROSS` (another company's recording named in `POST /browser-skill-drafts`' body: `inBody`), `company-scoped-sql.test.ts`, `jobs-schema.test.ts`, `company-archive.test.ts`. Each guard
mutation-checked.

## Letting a domain go, why a write run stopped, the Skill's steps as written (incident 2026-11-27)

A company Skill's write run on a real site stopped in the app at step 7 (0 writes, no permits) at the moment the network
to the server dropped. Its operator run's `finish` and the frames run's `end` never arrived: the operator run kept saying
`running` past its lease, the frames run stayed `open`, and the domain was refused to every new request (「別の AI の作業が
動いています」) until the frames run's 30 minutes. The card said one word for every stop, and the operator typed 〔〕 where the
Skill wrote 【】.

- **Ends lost to the network are sent again** (`client/electron/features/browser/pending-ends.ts`, one instance in
  `main.ts`, injected into `work.ts` and `browse.ts`): a frames run's `end` and an operator run's `finish` that got **no
  answer** (`sendEnd`) are kept in main's memory per member × company × run and sent again with back-off (2 s, 5 s, 15 s,
  30 s, then every minute) until the server answers — **any** answer settles it: an ended run's `end` answers the run, a
  second `finish` is refused `BROWSER_RUN_ENDED` and changes nothing (both idempotent on the server) — or until the run's
  own time is over (a frames run: its `expiresAt`; an operator run: now + its lease), after which the server frees the
  domain by itself. **Before the member's next task** on the company (`browser_work`, `browser_browse`), its kept ends
  are sent once more and awaited, before anything is started. Sign-out drops them — also an end whose first try was
  still on its way then (a generation counter, Codex review) — so another account's session never carries them. An end is one per run id; the body is the one first sent (the `site-refused` fallback included).
- **The server lets a Skill run's domain go when its work is over** (`services/browser-frames.ts` `settle`, asked by
  `holderOn`, start, permit, report, get and end; `BrowserFrameDeps.workRunOver` → `services/browser-runs.ts`
  `workRunOver`, wired in `composition.ts`): a Skill run allows exactly one work run (`BROWSER_RUN_WORK_TAKEN`, "Room for
  a Skill run"), so once that `skill` operator run is over — finished, refused, timed out, **or past its lease or hard
  end** — an `open` Skill run with **no write `permitted`** can permit nothing that matters: it ends at once with the
  fixed reason `work-ended` (`WORK_ENDED`) and the domain is free. **Never**: a `waiting` run (its card is up; a preview's
  run is `standard`, not the work), a `locked` run (a write `unknown` keeps the domain to the hour, as before), a run with
  a write `permitted` (it may be on its way: held until recorded or timed out), an open run whose work has not started
  yet, an ad-hoc plan's run (no once-rule there: held to its hour as before — decision, safe side).
- **Past its lease = ended, everywhere** (`services/browser-runs.ts`): a `running` operator run past its lease or hard end
  is ended `timed_out` on every touch — the lock (`runningOn`, a start), a continue, the finish, the list (`GET
  /browser-runs`) and `workRunOver` — so nothing reads it as still holding the domain.
- **Busy says when it frees up**: `BROWSER_RUN_BUSY` from the frames run's start and the operator run's start carries
  `freeAt` (ISO, additive; absent when unknown or from an older server): the latest the holder can keep the domain — a
  frames run's end by its times, an operator run's hard end, an ended run's in-flight write's permit + 35 s. The app takes
  only that ISO shape (`lines-api.ts`) and the consultation AI hears `BROWSER_WORK_WORDS.busyFor(n)` 「長くても約n分で空きます」
  (`workBusyWords`, whole minutes rounded up), else `busy` as before. An upper bound: with the rule above it frees sooner.
- **Why a write run stopped** (`WORK_STOP_REASONS`, `BROWSER_WORK_WORDS.stoppedBy`, the card's headline and the AI's
  sentence alike; fixed words, never page text): `not-found`, `need-login` (the operator's new stop word for a login page;
  `BROWSER_BROWSE_STOPS`, the run log's end reason too), `need-person` (the operator stopped: a person must decide),
  `blocked`, `refused` (a write outside the plan), `not-approved`, `network` (the server out of reach: a continue
  unanswered, a run's start unanswered), `stop-button` (the member's stop **or** the consultation closed: both end the
  turn's request and the app cannot tell them apart — said as one), `ai-stopped` (the AI stop, asked first: an
  administrator's stop also ends the turn), `timeout` (the operating time, or the server ending the run on its time),
  `rounds`, `window-closed`, `context-changed` (the app's sign-in, company or setting), `operator` (the operator AI did
  not answer in its form), `server-refused` (a continue refused: the setting, a level, this PC). The catch-all
  「途中で止めました（…など）」 is gone from the write run (the read-only `browser_browse` keeps its own words for now).
- **Who read what, on the cards**: the plan card's 「下見で読んだ大事な値」 says the operator AI read them from the page, that
  the consultation AI does not get them, and that they are not machine-checked; the result card's summary is 「ブラウザ操作
  の係の AI のまとめ」 saying the operator wrote it and the consultation AI does not get it (only when it finished: a stop has
  none), its checks 「ブラウザ操作の係の AI が確かめたこと」 and the row label `BROWSER_CARD_CHECKED_BY_WORDS.ai`
  「係の AI が確かめた（機械ではない）」 — "not machine-checked" is each row's own mark plus one folded line per card ("The
  stopped card asks one thing"), never repeated in headings or a note; the read-only operator's card says the same; the
  card body's heading 「サイトから読み取った情報（相談の AI には渡していません）」 (who read each part is said by its own
  heading or row); a line inspection's (machine) 「アプリがサイトから読み取った情報」.
- **The Skill's steps as written** (`workMaterial`; since "The operator works from the approved steps" the keys are
  `person`, `steps`, `plan`, `request`): the page (address, title, text, element names, values, options), the
  item headings and the operator's own notes stay outside text (`{ outside }`, 【】 → 〔〕). **A company Skill's steps**
  (its `SKILL.md` and `site-notes.md`, the sealed text) go as `steps`, the person's words as `person`, and (ad hoc only)
  the consultation AI's request as `request`, **as written** — no bracket
  neutralization: the values the operator types come from them, never from the page. Each is its own JSON value (the
  encoding is the fence: nothing inside a value can end it, which is also why neutralizing brackets inside JSON guarded
  nothing). No Skill → `steps: null`. Both prompts say: what to do and the values to type come from them,
  as written, 【】 included; `outside` text is data, and its 〔〕 may be 【】 in the original. Checked elsewhere: no other
  company text reaches the operator neutralized (the frames, after-read and never-read patterns are plain values; the
  read-only operator has no Skill steps; its task stays `outside` — it types only search words).
- **The model** (`FramesLife.tla`, README): unchanged — `work-ended` is `RunEnds` with a narrower guard (open, nothing
  permitted), a resent `end` is an idempotent `RunEnds`; the lock across runs is left out as before. 264,480 states.

Tests: `server/tests/unit/browser-frames.test.ts` "letting a domain go" (the incident: busy with `freeAt` until the work
run's lease runs out, then free at once, the frames run `work-ended`, the operator run `timed_out` in the list, a late
permit `BROWSER_RUN_ENDED`; a finished work run frees at once and a later `end` changes nothing; a write permitted holds
until recorded, an unknown one to the hour; a pre-approved run before its work, a waiting card after its preview and an
ad-hoc plan's work all held; both starts' busy `freeAt`), `server/tests/unit/browser-runs.test.ts` (a run past its lease
listed `timed_out` with no other touch, `runningOn` null, `workRunOver` false with no work run),
`client/tests/browser-domain-release.test.ts` (back-off until answered; a refusal settles; the run's own time stops the
tries; flush only this member's and company's, awaited; sign-out drops all; the incident wired: both ends kept and sent
before the next task's frames run; each stop's words in the card and the AI's sentence, all distinct; busy minutes and
only an ISO `freeAt` taken; a Skill step and the task with 【】 reach the operator unchanged while the page's text and the
notes are neutralized; the prompts; the card's who-read-what), `client/tests/ui/browser-inspection-card.spec.ts` (the
headings). Each guard mutation-checked.

## The operator's trail on the card (owner 2026-11-27: a stopped run showed nobody what the operator tried)

A write task's preview stopped with `need-person` and nobody — the member, the owner, the FDE tuning the Skill's steps —
could see what the operator AI had tried or why it stopped: the run log keeps actions and kept-form labels only, and a
preview that ended without a plan made **no card at all**. Now the person's card carries the operator's own trail.

- **The answer format** (both operators' prompts, `OPERATOR_REASON_LINE`): any answer may carry `reason` — its short
  reason for this step, for stopping, for finishing or for its plan (`stop`'s `why` stays the fixed word, so the free
  text has its own key). `operatorReason` (`client/shared/browser-browse.ts`, also used by `parseWorkAnswer`): absent →
  none; a string → one line, cut at 200 (a long reason costs its end, never the run); anything else → the answer is not
  in its form (`reason`, the run ends as for any bad answer). `notes` are now shown on the card too (the prompts say so).
  `unlisted` (what it sees on the screen with no ref) follows the same rule and shows in the memo ("The operator sees the
  screen").
  Inner objects of an answer (a plan's values, a done's checks) take neither key.
- **One place** (`client/shared/browser-operator-trail.ts`): `createOperatorTrail(phase)` records, per action, the run
  log's own step (`browseLogStep`: the page pattern, the action and the label **in the recording's kept form** — a row's
  text never) plus the operator's reason; its own end (`done` / `plan` / `stop` + reason; none when the app ended the
  run); its last notes. Caps (`BROWSER_TRAIL_LIMITS`): reason 200, one line, no control / bidirectional / zero-width
  character (`trailLine` = `cleanLabel`); notes 4000 (the answer's cap), line breaks kept (`trailNotes`); 60 steps shown, the rest counted;
  2 runs per card. `trailForCard` leaves out a run that did nothing. `readBrowserOperatorTrail` is the renderer's check:
  a trail off its shape or caps is left off the view (`readBrowserInspectionView`, `readBrowserWriteView`), the card
  still shows.
- **Where it shows**: `BrowserInspectionView.trail` (a read run's `bo_` card, `browse.ts`) and `BrowserWriteView.trail`
  (a write run's `bf_` card, `work.ts`: the preview's run and the work's, read live while the card is up). **A write
  task's preview that ends without a plan** (the operator's stop, or the app ending it — rounds, a bad answer, a stop
  button…) now keeps a card named by the preview's operator run (`bo_`, `workPreviewStoppedCard`: the fixed `stoppedBy`
  word + 「サイトには何も書いていません。」, no note — since "The stopped card asks one thing" its question or the window goes
  first) with the trail, in the work wiring's memory (`previewCard`; main's `runCard` asks the
  browse wiring first, then it), and puts its reference into the reply. A read run's endings without `done` / `stop`
  still make no card (as before).
- **Outside text, the person's card only**: the operator reads page text, so everything it wrote is treated as outside
  text — never in the consultation AI's words (`browseWordsForAi`, `workWordsForAi` are fixed words), never in the
  server's run log (the finish body: steps and writes as before), never on disk (main's memory with its card, gone at
  quit and sign-out; the renderer's stores are memory only; the chat keeps only the card's reference). The card
  (`OperatorTrail.tsx`) shows it **collapsed** under ［係の AI のメモを見る］, labelled 「ブラウザ操作の係の AI のメモ（係の AI が
  書いたもの。確かめた事実ではありません）」 (`BROWSER_TRAIL_WORDS`), as React text only (no markup, no Markdown): per step
  the action's word, the label, the page pattern and the reason; 「止めたときの係の AI の言葉」 (left out when it is exactly
  the question the card already puts first); the notes. The fixed reason word stays the card's one reason.
- **An answer out of form** (production trial 2026-11-27: the operator found the folder and the edit page, then a
  sensible plan was refused as `bad-answer` for a slip of form). Three parts:
  - **Lenient where it is safe** (`parseWorkAnswer`'s plan): the card's words are **cut** to their caps with an ellipsis
    (`summary` 1000, `affects` 500, a value's `label` 40 and `value` 200) instead of refusing the plan, and a Skill run's
    `writes: null` reads as none (its frames are the server's). Still strict: unknown keys (`plan-keys`), every type, an
    empty summary, the `unread` numbers, more than 10 values, a value that is not `{ label, value }`, an ad-hoc plan's
    writes (null, empty or anything off: `plan-writes`, checked by the declaration's own rules as before), a Skill plan
    naming writes (an empty list included), and every action, `done` and `stop` rule.
  - **One re-ask** (`work.ts`): when an answer fails the parser, the operator is asked again once — the next of the run's
    steps (its own `continue`, within the budget; never past it: at the last step the run ends on the slip) on the page as
    it is then — with `retry` in its material: the app's fixed sentence naming the rule (`workRetrySentence`,
    `BROWSER_ANSWER_PROBLEM_WORDS`), never the operator's own text. A second slip in a row ends the run (`bad-answer`); a
    good answer in between resets it (one re-ask per slip). The read run (`browse.ts`) is unchanged.
  - **Named on the card**: the run's trail records the rule (`BrowserTrailRun.problem`, a fixed code of
    `BROWSER_ANSWER_PROBLEMS`; the renderer's check takes nothing else) and the card says
    「アプリが止めました：係の AI の答えの形が決まりに合いませんでした（<the rule's fixed words>）。」 in place of the bare
    「アプリが作業を終えました」.
- **Remaining limits** (said): the reason and notes may hold what the page showed (names, values) — the card shows them
  to the member who ran the task, as the operator's summary already was; a stopped preview's `bo_` card opens in the
  main window only (the inspection card's IPC), not in a consultation's own window.

Tests: `client/tests/browser-operator-trail.test.ts` (a read run's steps, labels — a row's text never —, reasons, stop
words and notes on the card and never in the AI's words or the finish body; the incident: a stopped preview, ad hoc and
with a Skill, leaves a `bo_` card with the fixed word and the trail, another member's none, cleared on sign-out; a write
run's card with the preview's and the work's trails; the caps, unsafe characters, a non-string reason refused; the
prompts; the renderer's check leaving a bad trail off while the card stays), `src/features/browser-plans/components/
OperatorTrail.test.ts` (collapsed, labelled, nothing of it before the disclosure, markup escaped; the broken rule in fixed
words), `browser-frame-writes.test.ts` (a Skill plan's `writes: null` taken, an ad-hoc one's refused; overlong words cut;
keys, types, numbers and too many values still refused, each naming its rule; one re-ask with the fixed sentence and
never the slip itself, each answer one of the run's steps; a second slip ends the run with the rule on the trail; no
re-ask past the budget), `browser-operator-trail.test.ts` (a preview ended on two slips leaves a card naming the rule; the
renderer takes only a known code). Each guard mutation-checked (the trail on both cards, the stopped preview's card, the reason per step, every cap, the unsafe
characters, the renderer's line / notes / runs checks, the collapsed disclosure).

## The stopped card asks one thing (owner 2026-11-27: 「最悪の UX」)

A Skill that makes a 流入経路 stopped because the operator wanted to ask 「同名のタグが既にある。既存のタグを使ってよいか？」.
The app threw the question away (the work's `stop` had no key for it; it survived only as the stop's `reason` in the folded
memo), said why it stopped three times (the headline, a note, the memo), showed 「読み取れませんでした」 and 「機械で確かめた値
ではありません」 for nothing, and had no button: the member had to type a new request.

- **The question** (`parseWorkAnswer`'s `stop`, both write prompts: `WORK_STOP_LINE` and the `need-person` line):
  `{"do":"stop","why":"need-person",…,"question":"<人に決めてほしいこと>"}` — optional; a string is made one clean line
  (`stopQuestion` = `trailLine` at `BROWSER_TRAIL_LIMITS.question` 300: NFC, no control / bidirectional / zero-width
  character, cut, never refused), null / absent = none, anything else refuses the answer (`stop`). An operator that put it
  in its stop `reason` (all of them before this key) is still heard: `work.ts` `operatorQuestion` = the question, else
  the trail's stop reason.
- **Only the operator's own stop asks** (`workStopNext`, one place): `need-person` with a question → `{ kind: 'decide',
  question }` **only when the run held no page write**; after any held write → `{ kind: 'question', question }`, shown
  first **without** the answers (Codex review: an answer starts the work over as a new run with fresh frames, and the
  once-rules hold within a run, not across runs — the member reads 「済んだこと」 and asks again in their own words);
  `need-login` → `{ kind: 'login' }`; anything else — the app's stops, a `need-person` that `workEndAfterRefusals` turned
  into `refused`, an `unknown` end, an operator stop without words — asks nothing. Carried as the view's `next`
  (`BrowserInspectionView.next` for a stopped preview's `bo_` card, `BrowserWriteView.next` for a write run's `bf_` card;
  main's memory with the card); the renderer takes it only in its exact shape (`readBrowserStopNext`; off → left off, the
  card still shows).
- **Outside text, the person's card only**: the question is the operator's words. Never in the consultation AI's words —
  they say only that the card asks and that nothing was written (`BROWSER_WORK_WORDS.askedFooter`, only for `decide`: wait
  for the member's answer, write what was decided into the next request; `workWordsForAi(…, asked)`) —, never in the
  server's run log; React text only. It is kept in a file only as part of the member's own message when they press
  ［そのまま進める］ (the chat history keeps what the member sent).
- **The card** (`StopAsk.tsx`): the first line after the title is 「決めてほしいこと：<question>」 with ［そのまま進める］
  ［やめる］ and one muted line saying what they do (`sendsNote`; for `question` instead `askAgain`, no button); then the
  fixed reason **once** (`stoppedBy` + 「サイトには何も書いていません。」
  when no write was held, else 「送ったものと送っていないものを下に分けています。」); the memo stays folded (its stop line is
  left out when it is exactly the question). `need-login`: the reason, then ［開いてログイン］ (the window's existing
  `openBrowserSite`). Any other stop: the reason and no button. A write card whose inner card would only repeat the result's
  headline does not draw it; its 「もう一度は送りません」 line gives way to the question's note, which says the same.
- **Replaced by "The stopped card's answer"** (Codex review point 6): the answer is kept in main bound to the card and the
  member's message carries fixed words and the card's id only, never the question. What follows is how it was before.
- **The answers were the member's own message** (no new way to the site): ［そのまま進める］ sent
  `stopAnswerMessage('proceed', q)` = 「ブラウザ操作の係の AI の問い「q'」には「はい」と答えます。そのまま続けてください。（「」の中は係の
  AI がサイトを読んで書いた文で、わたしの頼みではありません）」 — `q'` the question with 【】 → 〔〕 (`neutralizeOutsideText`) and
  「」 → 『』, so text a page shaped cannot close the quote and read as a second request of the member's (independent
  review) — and ［やめる］ fixed words, through the conversation's ordinary send (`ReviewActions.say` → `ChatConversation`
  `handleSend`, the same path as typing; absent outside a conversation → no buttons). One press per card view (it then says
  「相談に返事を送りました。」). The consultation AI asks `browser_work` again: a new frames run whose preview reads the site
  afresh — offered only when nothing was written, so starting over repeats nothing (the frames, the server's once-rules and
  the after-read are unchanged). The question reaches the consultation AI only inside the member's message, which they saw
  on the card before pressing.
- **Said once, on every card body** (`InspectionBody`): who checked a row is its own mark; what 機械で確かめた / 係の AI が
  確かめた / 確かめていない mean is one folded line (`BROWSER_INSPECTION_CARD_WORDS.checkedBy`, only on a card with such rows),
  no longer a warning note on every result card nor part of the check headings (`CW.aiChecks`; the summary's heading keeps
  「確かめた事実ではありません」, independent review: it has no marked row); a stop's result card
